# Business Vocabulary Upgrade Book

**Basic words → Advanced business replacements, with meanings and examples**

2000 common, everyday words paired with a more sophisticated, professional replacement used in business and corporate English — e.g. *show → demonstrate*, *use → utilize*, *big → substantial*.

Grouped alphabetically by the basic word, from most to least commonly used within each group.

---

## Section: A – C

### 1. about → approximately

**Meaning:** close to a number, not exact

**Examples:**
1. We expect approximately 200 attendees at the annual conference.
2. The project will take approximately six weeks to complete.

---

### 2. big → substantial

**Meaning:** large in size, amount, or importance

**Examples:**
1. We secured a substantial increase in our marketing budget this quarter.
2. The client made a substantial investment in our new platform.

---

### 3. buy → procure

**Meaning:** to obtain something, usually through a formal purchasing process

**Examples:**
1. Procurement will procure the new laptops through our approved vendor.
2. We plan to procure office supplies directly from the manufacturer.

---

### 4. change → modify

**Meaning:** to alter or adjust something

**Examples:**
1. We need to modify the proposal before sending it to the board.
2. The vendor agreed to modify the delivery schedule at our request.

---

### 5. check → verify

**Meaning:** to confirm that something is correct or true

**Examples:**
1. Please verify the figures before we submit the report.
2. Finance will verify each expense claim before reimbursement.

---

### 6. call → contact

**Meaning:** to get in touch with someone

**Examples:**
1. Please contact the vendor to confirm the shipment date.
2. I will contact the client tomorrow morning to schedule a follow-up.

---

### 7. close → finalize

**Meaning:** to complete something so it is settled and done

**Examples:**
1. We aim to finalize the contract by the end of the week.
2. Let's finalize the agenda before the meeting begins.

---

### 8. cost → expenditure

**Meaning:** money spent on something

**Examples:**
1. Total expenditure for the campaign exceeded our initial forecast.
2. The finance team tracks monthly expenditure across all departments.

---

### 9. create → generate

**Meaning:** to produce or bring something into existence

**Examples:**
1. The new pricing model should generate additional revenue.
2. Our team will generate a detailed report for the stakeholders.

---

### 10. cut → reduce

**Meaning:** to make something smaller in amount

**Examples:**
1. We need to reduce operating costs by ten percent this year.
2. The committee voted to reduce the project timeline by two weeks.

---

### 11. ask → inquire

**Meaning:** to request information politely

**Examples:**
1. I would like to inquire about the status of our order.
2. The client called to inquire about extending the contract.

---

### 12. answer → respond

**Meaning:** to reply to a question or message

**Examples:**
1. Please respond to the client's email by end of day.
2. We will respond to all feedback within 48 hours.

---

### 13. agree → concur

**Meaning:** to have the same opinion as someone else

**Examples:**
1. The board members concur with the proposed budget changes.
2. I concur with your assessment of the market risks.

---

### 14. allow → permit

**Meaning:** to let something happen or give permission

**Examples:**
1. Company policy does not permit remote access to that server.
2. The new schedule will permit greater flexibility for staff.

---

### 15. add → incorporate

**Meaning:** to include something as part of a whole

**Examples:**
1. We will incorporate your feedback into the final draft.
2. The design team incorporated the new branding guidelines.

---

### 16. also → additionally

**Meaning:** as well as what has already been mentioned

**Examples:**
1. Additionally, the report highlights a rise in customer churn.
2. The vendor will additionally provide training for the new system.

---

### 17. always → consistently

**Meaning:** happening every time, without fail

**Examples:**
1. The team has consistently met its quarterly targets.
2. Our supplier consistently delivers on time.

---

### 18. area → domain

**Meaning:** a particular field of activity or expertise

**Examples:**
1. Data privacy falls within the legal team's domain.
2. We are expanding into a new domain of the market.

---

### 19. amount → quantity

**Meaning:** how much of something there is

**Examples:**
1. The quantity of raw materials ordered exceeds last month's total.
2. We need to confirm the quantity before placing the order.

---

### 20. avoid → circumvent

**Meaning:** to find a way around a problem or rule

**Examples:**
1. The team found a workaround to circumvent the software limitation.
2. We must not circumvent standard approval procedures.

---

### 21. aware → cognizant

**Meaning:** knowing about or realizing something

**Examples:**
1. Please be cognizant of the new compliance requirements.
2. Management is cognizant of the risks involved in this merger.

---

### 22. begin → commence

**Meaning:** to start something

**Examples:**
1. The onboarding process will commence next Monday.
2. Construction on the new office is set to commence in July.

---

### 23. best → optimal

**Meaning:** the most favorable or effective option

**Examples:**
1. We identified the optimal pricing strategy for the launch.
2. This is the optimal time to enter the market.

---

### 24. better → superior

**Meaning:** of higher quality or standard

**Examples:**
1. Our product offers superior performance compared to competitors.
2. The new supplier provides superior customer service.

---

### 25. bring → deliver

**Meaning:** to provide or supply something

**Examples:**
1. The agency promised to deliver measurable results within a quarter.
2. We will deliver the final assets by Friday.

---

### 26. build → construct

**Meaning:** to create or put something together

**Examples:**
1. The team will construct a detailed roadmap for the next fiscal year.
2. We need to construct a stronger business case for the investment.

---

### 27. busy → occupied

**Meaning:** engaged in activity or unavailable

**Examples:**
1. The conference room is occupied until three o'clock.
2. I'm currently occupied with the quarterly audit.

---

### 28. back → endorse

**Meaning:** to support or approve of something

**Examples:**
1. The executive team decided to endorse the new expansion plan.
2. Several investors have chosen to endorse the merger.

---

### 29. bad → substandard

**Meaning:** below an acceptable level of quality

**Examples:**
1. The vendor's delivery was flagged for substandard packaging.
2. We rejected the shipment due to substandard materials.

---

### 30. care → attend to

**Meaning:** to deal with or handle something carefully

**Examples:**
1. Please attend to the client's concerns before the review call.
2. HR will attend to the outstanding onboarding paperwork.

---

### 31. careful → meticulous

**Meaning:** paying close attention to detail

**Examples:**
1. The audit team was meticulous in reviewing every transaction.
2. Her meticulous planning kept the launch on schedule.

---

### 32. clear → unambiguous

**Meaning:** leaving no room for doubt or confusion

**Examples:**
1. The contract terms must be unambiguous to avoid disputes.
2. Please provide unambiguous instructions to the offshore team.

---

### 33. clean → streamline

**Meaning:** to make a process simpler and more efficient

**Examples:**
1. We plan to streamline the approval workflow this quarter.
2. The new software will streamline invoice processing.

---

### 34. common → prevalent

**Meaning:** widely found or occurring often

**Examples:**
1. Remote work has become prevalent across the industry.
2. This issue is prevalent among mid-sized manufacturers.

---

### 35. complete → accomplish

**Meaning:** to successfully finish something

**Examples:**
1. The team managed to accomplish all project milestones on time.
2. We accomplished our sales target a month early.

---

### 36. control → regulate

**Meaning:** to manage or direct something according to rules

**Examples:**
1. New policies will regulate how expenses are approved.
2. The finance department regulates cash flow across divisions.

---

### 37. cover → encompass

**Meaning:** to include a range of things

**Examples:**
1. The new policy will encompass all remote employees.
2. Our service package encompasses training and support.

---

### 38. choose → select

**Meaning:** to pick one option from several

**Examples:**
1. The committee will select a vendor by next Friday.
2. Please select the option that best fits our budget.

---

### 39. continue → persist

**Meaning:** to keep doing something without stopping

**Examples:**
1. Sales growth is expected to persist through the next quarter.
2. The team will persist with the current strategy for now.

---

### 40. correct → rectify

**Meaning:** to fix an error or problem

**Examples:**
1. We need to rectify the billing discrepancy immediately.
2. The vendor agreed to rectify the shipping error at no cost.

---

### 41. count → tally

**Meaning:** to add up a total number

**Examples:**
1. Please tally the votes from the shareholder meeting.
2. The system automatically tallies daily transaction totals.

---

### 42. curious → inquisitive

**Meaning:** eager to learn or know more

**Examples:**
1. The interviewer noted her inquisitive approach to problem-solving.
2. Clients appreciate an inquisitive consultant who asks the right questions.

---

### 43. current → prevailing

**Meaning:** existing or happening now

**Examples:**
1. Our strategy must adapt to prevailing market conditions.
2. The prevailing interest rate affects our borrowing costs.

---

### 44. confirm → corroborate

**Meaning:** to support or verify a claim with evidence

**Examples:**
1. The audit findings corroborate our earlier revenue projections.
2. We need additional data to corroborate the client's complaint.

---

### 45. connect → liaise

**Meaning:** to communicate and cooperate with others

**Examples:**
1. Please liaise with the legal team before signing the agreement.
2. Our regional manager will liaise with the overseas office.

---

### 46. consider → contemplate

**Meaning:** to think carefully about something

**Examples:**
1. The board will contemplate the acquisition offer next week.
2. We should contemplate alternative suppliers before renewing the contract.

---

### 47. company → organization

**Meaning:** a group of people working together for a business purpose

**Examples:**
1. Our organization values transparency and accountability.
2. The organization plans to expand into three new markets.

---

### 48. accept → embrace

**Meaning:** to willingly adopt or welcome something

**Examples:**
1. Leadership has decided to embrace a hybrid work model.
2. The team was quick to embrace the new project management tool.

---

### 49. access → obtain

**Meaning:** to get or acquire something

**Examples:**
1. Employees can obtain the reports through the internal portal.
2. We need to obtain approval before releasing the funds.

---

### 50. act → proceed

**Meaning:** to move forward with an action or plan

**Examples:**
1. Once approved, we will proceed with the rollout immediately.
2. The legal team advised us to proceed with caution.

---

### 51. address → tackle

**Meaning:** to deal with a problem directly

**Examples:**
1. The task force was formed to tackle rising customer complaints.
2. We must tackle the supply chain delays before the holiday season.

---

### 52. adjust → recalibrate

**Meaning:** to make small changes to improve accuracy or fit

**Examples:**
1. We need to recalibrate our sales forecast for the new quarter.
2. The team recalibrated the project timeline after the delay.

---

### 53. admit → acknowledge

**Meaning:** to accept that something is true

**Examples:**
1. Management acknowledged the shortfall in the latest earnings call.
2. We should acknowledge the client's concerns in our response.

---

### 54. afford → sustain

**Meaning:** to be able to maintain something over time

**Examples:**
1. The company cannot sustain this level of spending indefinitely.
2. Our current margins allow us to sustain the discount program.

---

### 55. after → subsequent to

**Meaning:** following or coming after something else

**Examples:**
1. Subsequent to the merger, both teams will report to one director.
2. The rollout will begin subsequent to final testing.

---

### 56. again → reiterate

**Meaning:** to say something again for emphasis

**Examples:**
1. I want to reiterate the importance of meeting this deadline.
2. The CEO reiterated the company's commitment to sustainability.

---

### 57. aim → target

**Meaning:** a goal that someone is trying to achieve

**Examples:**
1. Our target is to increase market share by five percent.
2. The sales team is working toward an ambitious quarterly target.

---

### 58. alone → independently

**Meaning:** without help from others

**Examples:**
1. Each regional office operates independently within the budget guidelines.
2. She completed the audit independently before the deadline.

---

### 59. amaze → astonish

**Meaning:** to surprise someone greatly

**Examples:**
1. The rapid growth figures managed to astonish the board.
2. The product demo astonished potential investors.

---

### 60. appear → emerge

**Meaning:** to come into view or become known

**Examples:**
1. New competitors continue to emerge in the fintech space.
2. A clear pattern emerged from the customer survey data.

---

### 61. argue → contend

**Meaning:** to state a position firmly and give reasons for it

**Examples:**
1. The analyst contended that the market is overvalued.
2. Legal counsel contends that the clause is unenforceable.

---

### 62. arrive → materialize

**Meaning:** to become real or actually happen

**Examples:**
1. The anticipated cost savings failed to materialize this quarter.
2. If the partnership materializes, it could double our revenue.

---

### 63. assist → facilitate

**Meaning:** to make a process easier by helping

**Examples:**
1. The consultant will facilitate discussions between both departments.
2. This software facilitates faster onboarding for new employees.

---

### 64. attend → participate

**Meaning:** to take part in an activity or event

**Examples:**
1. All department heads are expected to participate in the retreat.
2. Employees are encouraged to participate in the wellness program.

---

### 65. available → accessible

**Meaning:** able to be reached, used, or obtained

**Examples:**
1. The dashboard is accessible to all senior managers.
2. We are making the training materials accessible online.

---

### 66. awful → abysmal

**Meaning:** extremely bad or poor

**Examples:**
1. Customer satisfaction scores were abysmal after the outage.
2. The vendor's response time has been abysmal this month.

---

### 67. awkward → uncomfortable

**Meaning:** causing unease or difficulty

**Examples:**
1. The layoffs created an uncomfortable atmosphere in the office.
2. It was an uncomfortable conversation, but a necessary one.

---

### 68. accurate → precise

**Meaning:** exact and free from error

**Examples:**
1. We require precise figures before submitting the tax filing.
2. The forecasting model produces increasingly precise estimates.

---

### 69. actual → genuine

**Meaning:** real, not fake or exaggerated

**Examples:**
1. We need a genuine assessment of the project's risks.
2. The client wants genuine feedback, not just praise.

---

### 70. adopt → implement

**Meaning:** to put a plan or system into action

**Examples:**
1. The company will implement the new expense policy in January.
2. We plan to implement automated reporting across all branches.

---

### 71. advice → counsel

**Meaning:** guidance or recommendations given to someone

**Examples:**
1. We sought counsel from an external consultant before the merger.
2. Legal counsel advised against signing the amended agreement.

---

### 72. afraid → apprehensive

**Meaning:** worried or anxious about something

**Examples:**
1. Investors remained apprehensive about the volatile market.
2. The team was apprehensive about the tight deadline.

---

### 73. aid → bolster

**Meaning:** to strengthen or support something

**Examples:**
1. The new hires will bolster our customer support capacity.
2. The grant is intended to bolster small business growth.

---

### 74. amend → revise

**Meaning:** to change something in order to improve or correct it

**Examples:**
1. We need to revise the contract before both parties sign.
2. The policy was revised after employee feedback.

---

### 75. analyze → scrutinize

**Meaning:** to examine something closely and carefully

**Examples:**
1. Auditors will scrutinize every expense report this quarter.
2. The board scrutinized the acquisition terms before approval.

---

### 76. apply → utilize

**Meaning:** to make use of something for a purpose

**Examples:**
1. We should utilize existing resources before hiring more staff.
2. The team utilized customer data to refine the marketing strategy.

---

### 77. approach → methodology

**Meaning:** a systematic way of doing something

**Examples:**
1. Our methodology for the audit follows industry best practices.
2. The consultants proposed a new methodology for data collection.

---

### 78. attach → append

**Meaning:** to add something to a document or item

**Examples:**
1. Please append the invoice to your expense report.
2. I have appended the revised terms to this email.

---

### 79. attempt → endeavor

**Meaning:** an effort to do something

**Examples:**
1. The team's endeavor to cut costs paid off within months.
2. We will endeavor to resolve the issue by tomorrow.

---

### 80. attract → entice

**Meaning:** to draw interest or attention to something

**Examples:**
1. The discount was designed to entice first-time buyers.
2. A strong benefits package helps entice top talent.

---

### 81. announce → proclaim

**Meaning:** to state something publicly and officially

**Examples:**
1. The CEO will proclaim the merger at tomorrow's press conference.
2. The company proclaimed record profits in its annual report.

---

### 82. annoy → irritate

**Meaning:** to make someone slightly angry

**Examples:**
1. Repeated system outages have begun to irritate our clients.
2. Delayed responses tend to irritate stakeholders.

---

### 83. anxious → perturbed

**Meaning:** feeling disturbed or worried

**Examples:**
1. Shareholders were perturbed by the sudden drop in stock price.
2. Employees seemed perturbed by the unannounced reorganization.

---

### 84. apologize → express regret

**Meaning:** to say sorry for a mistake or fault

**Examples:**
1. We wish to express regret for the delay in shipment.
2. The manager expressed regret over the miscommunication.

---

### 85. appoint → designate

**Meaning:** to officially choose someone for a role

**Examples:**
1. The board will designate a new chief financial officer.
2. She was designated as project lead for the merger.

---

### 86. approve → sanction

**Meaning:** to officially agree to or authorize something

**Examples:**
1. The committee sanctioned the additional budget request.
2. Management has yet to sanction the new hiring plan.

---

### 87. arrange → coordinate

**Meaning:** to organize the details of an event or plan

**Examples:**
1. Please coordinate the logistics for the client visit.
2. HR is coordinating the schedule for next week's interviews.

---

### 88. assign → allocate

**Meaning:** to give a specific task or resource to someone

**Examples:**
1. We will allocate additional staff to the customer support team.
2. The manager allocated the budget across three departments.

---

### 89. assume → presume

**Meaning:** to accept something as true without proof

**Examples:**
1. We should not presume the client will renew automatically.
2. The forecast presumes stable interest rates through the year.

---

### 90. alike → comparable

**Meaning:** similar in nature or quality

**Examples:**
1. The two proposals are comparable in cost and scope.
2. Both vendors offer comparable service levels.

---

### 91. alert → flag

**Meaning:** to draw attention to a potential issue

**Examples:**
1. Please flag any discrepancies in the quarterly report.
2. The system will flag transactions above the approved limit.

---

### 92. alter → restructure

**Meaning:** to reorganize the structure of something

**Examples:**
1. The company decided to restructure its regional sales teams.
2. We may need to restructure the repayment schedule.

---

### 93. base → underpin

**Meaning:** to form the foundation or support for something

**Examples:**
1. Strong customer data underpins our marketing strategy.
2. The forecast is underpinned by three years of sales history.

---

### 94. basic → fundamental

**Meaning:** forming an essential base or core part

**Examples:**
1. Data security is a fundamental requirement for this contract.
2. Trust is fundamental to any successful partnership.

---

### 95. beat → surpass

**Meaning:** to do better than a target or competitor

**Examples:**
1. The team surpassed its quarterly sales quota by 15 percent.
2. Our new product surpassed customer expectations at launch.

---

### 96. believe → maintain

**Meaning:** to state and hold firmly to an opinion

**Examples:**
1. Management maintains that the acquisition will boost profitability.
2. The analyst maintains that the market will recover by year-end.

---

### 97. boost → augment

**Meaning:** to increase or improve something

**Examples:**
1. The new hires will augment our engineering capacity.
2. Targeted ads are expected to augment brand visibility.

---

### 98. boring → tedious

**Meaning:** dull and requiring too much effort or time

**Examples:**
1. Manual data entry remains a tedious part of the process.
2. Automating the tedious reconciliation task saved hours each week.

---

### 99. boss → supervisor

**Meaning:** a person who manages and oversees employees

**Examples:**
1. Please loop in your supervisor before approving the change.
2. Her supervisor praised the report's thoroughness.

---

### 100. bother → inconvenience

**Meaning:** to cause someone trouble or difficulty

**Examples:**
1. We apologize for any inconvenience caused by the delay.
2. The reschedule will inconvenience several attendees.

---

### 101. break → disrupt

**Meaning:** to interrupt or interfere with something

**Examples:**
1. The outage disrupted operations across all regional offices.
2. A single supplier delay can disrupt the entire production line.

---

### 102. brief → concise

**Meaning:** giving information clearly in a few words

**Examples:**
1. Please keep the executive summary concise and to the point.
2. The presenter delivered a concise overview of the quarterly results.

---

### 103. broad → extensive

**Meaning:** covering a large area or range of topics

**Examples:**
1. The consultant has extensive experience in supply chain management.
2. Our research covered an extensive range of customer segments.

---

### 104. broken → defective

**Meaning:** not working properly due to a fault

**Examples:**
1. The defective units were returned to the manufacturer for credit.
2. Customers reported a defective batch of the latest model.

---

### 105. bunch → cluster

**Meaning:** a group of similar things gathered together

**Examples:**
1. We identified a cluster of complaints related to shipping delays.
2. The analysis revealed a cluster of high-value customers in one region.

---

### 106. balance → equilibrium

**Meaning:** a state of stability between different forces

**Examples:**
1. The team is working to find equilibrium between speed and quality.
2. Maintaining equilibrium between supply and demand is critical.

---

### 107. ban → prohibit

**Meaning:** to officially forbid something

**Examples:**
1. The new policy will prohibit the use of personal devices at work.
2. Regulators may prohibit the merger on antitrust grounds.

---

### 108. bargain → negotiate

**Meaning:** to discuss terms in order to reach an agreement

**Examples:**
1. Our procurement team will negotiate better rates with the supplier.
2. Both parties negotiated for weeks before signing the deal.

---

### 109. barrier → impediment

**Meaning:** something that blocks progress

**Examples:**
1. High tariffs remain a major impediment to entering that market.
2. Legacy systems are an impediment to our digital transformation.

---

### 110. behind → delayed

**Meaning:** not on schedule; late

**Examples:**
1. The shipment is delayed due to customs clearance issues.
2. Our launch has been delayed by two weeks.

---

### 111. blend → integrate

**Meaning:** to combine different things smoothly

**Examples:**
1. We plan to integrate the acquired company's systems by Q3.
2. The new hire quickly integrated into the marketing team.

---

### 112. bright → promising

**Meaning:** showing good signs for the future

**Examples:**
1. The startup's early metrics look promising to investors.
2. We see a promising outlook for the upcoming fiscal year.

---

### 113. broke → insolvent

**Meaning:** unable to pay debts owed

**Examples:**
1. The retailer was declared insolvent after months of losses.
2. Lenders grew concerned the company was becoming insolvent.

---

### 114. bulk → volume

**Meaning:** a large quantity of something

**Examples:**
1. We negotiated a discount for purchasing in volume.
2. The warehouse struggled to handle the sudden volume of orders.

---

### 115. burden → encumbrance

**Meaning:** something that weighs heavily or creates difficulty

**Examples:**
1. The outstanding debt is an encumbrance on the company's balance sheet.
2. Regulatory reporting has become a significant encumbrance for small firms.

---

### 116. benefit → advantage

**Meaning:** something that gives a favorable position

**Examples:**
1. Early adoption of the platform gives us a competitive advantage.
2. The partnership offers a clear advantage in distribution reach.

---

### 117. bit → fraction

**Meaning:** a small part of a whole

**Examples:**
1. Only a fraction of the budget has been spent so far.
2. A small fraction of customers reported issues with the update.

---

### 118. budget → allocation

**Meaning:** an amount of money set aside for a purpose

**Examples:**
1. The marketing allocation was increased for the product launch.
2. Each department received a revised allocation for next year.

---

### 119. brainstorm → ideate

**Meaning:** to generate new ideas as a group

**Examples:**
1. The design team will ideate solutions during tomorrow's workshop.
2. We spent the morning ideating new features for the app.

---

### 120. backup → contingency

**Meaning:** a plan prepared in case something goes wrong

**Examples:**
1. We have a contingency plan in case the primary supplier fails.
2. The IT team maintains a contingency for server outages.

---

### 121. breakdown → analysis

**Meaning:** a detailed examination of parts of something

**Examples:**
1. Please provide a full analysis of the quarterly expenses.
2. The report includes a cost analysis by department.

---

### 122. bug → anomaly

**Meaning:** an unexpected error or irregularity

**Examples:**
1. Engineers identified an anomaly in the payment processing system.
2. The QA team flagged an anomaly before the release went live.

---

### 123. buzz → momentum

**Meaning:** a growing sense of energy or interest

**Examples:**
1. The product launch generated strong momentum on social media.
2. We need to sustain this momentum through the next quarter.

---

### 124. bump → increment

**Meaning:** a small increase in amount or level

**Examples:**
1. Staff will receive a salary increment starting next month.
2. We requested a small increment to the project budget.

---

### 125. blame → attribute

**Meaning:** to say that something is caused by a particular thing

**Examples:**
1. The delay was attributed to a shortage of raw materials.
2. Management attributed the sales dip to seasonal factors.

---

### 126. bold → assertive

**Meaning:** confident and direct in approach

**Examples:**
1. The CEO took an assertive stance during merger negotiations.
2. An assertive marketing campaign helped the brand stand out.

---

### 127. calm → composed

**Meaning:** not showing anxiety or excitement

**Examples:**
1. The negotiator remained composed throughout the tense discussion.
2. Leadership stayed composed during the market downturn.

---

### 128. cancel → rescind

**Meaning:** to officially withdraw or cancel an agreement

**Examples:**
1. The client decided to rescind the purchase order last minute.
2. The board voted to rescind the previous policy.

---

### 129. capable → competent

**Meaning:** having the skills or ability to do something well

**Examples:**
1. The new hire proved competent in handling complex accounts.
2. We need a competent project manager to oversee the rollout.

---

### 130. careless → negligent

**Meaning:** failing to take proper care, resulting in harm

**Examples:**
1. The audit revealed negligent handling of sensitive customer data.
2. The vendor was found negligent in following safety protocols.

---

### 131. carry → transport

**Meaning:** to move goods or people from one place to another

**Examples:**
1. The logistics team will transport the equipment to the new site.
2. We use a third-party carrier to transport goods overseas.

---

### 132. catch → detect

**Meaning:** to discover or notice something

**Examples:**
1. The audit software helped detect several billing errors.
2. Our monitoring tools detected the outage within minutes.

---

### 133. cause → instigate

**Meaning:** to bring about or start something

**Examples:**
1. The price increase instigated a wave of customer complaints.
2. Poor communication instigated confusion among the teams.

---

### 134. central → pivotal

**Meaning:** of crucial importance to something

**Examples:**
1. Customer retention is pivotal to our long-term growth strategy.
2. This partnership played a pivotal role in our expansion.

---

### 135. certain → definitive

**Meaning:** clear and not open to doubt

**Examples:**
1. We need a definitive answer from legal before proceeding.
2. The report offers a definitive breakdown of quarterly performance.

---

### 136. chance → opportunity

**Meaning:** a favorable set of circumstances

**Examples:**
1. This partnership presents a strong opportunity for growth.
2. We see an opportunity to expand into the Asian market.

---

### 137. cheap → cost-effective

**Meaning:** providing good value for a low cost

**Examples:**
1. The new supplier offers a more cost-effective packaging solution.
2. Cloud storage proved more cost-effective than physical servers.

---

### 138. cheat → deceive

**Meaning:** to mislead someone deliberately

**Examples:**
1. The company was accused of trying to deceive investors.
2. Regulators fined the firm for attempting to deceive auditors.

---

### 139. chief → principal

**Meaning:** main or most important

**Examples:**
1. Rising costs remain the principal concern for shareholders.
2. The principal objective of this meeting is budget approval.

---

### 140. clever → ingenious

**Meaning:** cleverly designed or original in idea

**Examples:**
1. The team devised an ingenious solution to the shipping delay.
2. Investors were impressed by the startup's ingenious business model.

---

### 141. clumsy → inept

**Meaning:** lacking skill in performing a task

**Examples:**
1. The rollout was criticized as an inept handling of customer data.
2. An inept response to the complaint damaged the client relationship.

---

### 142. clutter → disarray

**Meaning:** a state of disorder or confusion

**Examples:**
1. The filing system fell into disarray after the office move.
2. Poor documentation left the project records in disarray.

---

### 143. coach → mentor

**Meaning:** to guide and train someone, often over time

**Examples:**
1. Senior managers are encouraged to mentor new employees.
2. She mentors junior analysts through their first year.

---

### 144. collect → compile

**Meaning:** to gather information or items together

**Examples:**
1. We need to compile customer feedback before the review meeting.
2. The team compiled data from all regional offices into one report.

---

### 145. combine → consolidate

**Meaning:** to bring separate things together into one

**Examples:**
1. We plan to consolidate our three warehouses into a single facility.
2. The finance team consolidated all regional budgets into one report.

---

### 146. comment → feedback

**Meaning:** opinions or reactions given about something

**Examples:**
1. We received valuable feedback from clients during the pilot phase.
2. Please share your feedback on the revised proposal by Friday.

---

### 147. commit → pledge

**Meaning:** to promise to do something

**Examples:**
1. The company pledged to reduce emissions by 20 percent by 2030.
2. Each department pledged to meet its cost-saving targets.

---

### 148. complicated → intricate

**Meaning:** having many interconnected parts, hard to understand

**Examples:**
1. The merger involved an intricate set of legal and financial steps.
2. The new tax regulations are notoriously intricate.

---

### 149. concern → apprehension

**Meaning:** a feeling of worry about something

**Examples:**
1. There is growing apprehension among staff about the restructuring.
2. Investors expressed apprehension over the delayed earnings report.

---

### 150. condition → stipulation

**Meaning:** a specific requirement in an agreement

**Examples:**
1. The contract includes a stipulation regarding late delivery penalties.
2. One stipulation of the loan is quarterly financial reporting.

---

### 151. constant → perpetual

**Meaning:** continuing without interruption

**Examples:**
1. The team operates under perpetual pressure to hit quarterly targets.
2. Perpetual system upgrades have strained the IT budget.

---

### 152. contact → correspond with

**Meaning:** to communicate with someone, especially in writing

**Examples:**
1. Please correspond with the legal department regarding the contract.
2. We will correspond with the supplier to confirm the new terms.

---

### 153. contain → comprise

**Meaning:** to consist of or include certain parts

**Examples:**
1. The proposal comprises three phases of implementation.
2. The report comprises data from five different markets.

---

### 154. convince → persuade

**Meaning:** to cause someone to believe or do something through argument

**Examples:**
1. The sales team worked hard to persuade the client to upgrade.
2. Strong data helped persuade the board to approve the budget.

---

### 155. cope → navigate

**Meaning:** to successfully deal with a difficult situation

**Examples:**
1. The company had to navigate significant supply chain disruptions.
2. Our team navigated the regulatory changes without major delays.

---

### 156. core → essence

**Meaning:** the most important or fundamental part of something

**Examples:**
1. The essence of our strategy is customer-first innovation.
2. Cost reduction is the essence of this restructuring plan.

---

### 157. corner → niche

**Meaning:** a specialized segment of a market

**Examples:**
1. The startup carved out a profitable niche in eco-friendly packaging.
2. We identified an untapped niche within the healthcare sector.

---

### 158. counter → refute

**Meaning:** to prove that a claim or statement is wrong

**Examples:**
1. The finance team refuted the auditor's initial findings.
2. Our legal counsel refuted the competitor's patent claim.

---

### 159. courage → fortitude

**Meaning:** strength in facing difficulty or challenge

**Examples:**
1. Leadership showed fortitude in pushing through the restructuring.
2. It took fortitude for the startup to pivot its business model.

---

### 160. crash → plummet

**Meaning:** to fall suddenly and sharply

**Examples:**
1. Shares in the company plummeted after the earnings miss.
2. Demand plummeted following the negative press coverage.

---

### 161. crazy → unconventional

**Meaning:** not following usual or traditional methods

**Examples:**
1. The startup's unconventional pricing model disrupted the industry.
2. Investors were initially skeptical of the unconventional strategy.

---

### 162. credit → accredit

**Meaning:** to officially recognize someone for an achievement

**Examples:**
1. The team should be accredited for exceeding the annual target.
2. Analysts accredited the turnaround to new leadership.

---

### 163. criticize → critique

**Meaning:** to give a detailed assessment, often pointing out flaws

**Examples:**
1. The panel critiqued the proposal before granting final approval.
2. Reviewers critiqued the product's outdated interface.

---

### 164. cure → resolve

**Meaning:** to find a solution to a problem

**Examples:**
1. The support team worked overnight to resolve the outage.
2. We resolved the billing dispute within two business days.

---

### 165. curve → trajectory

**Meaning:** the general path or direction something follows over time

**Examples:**
1. The company's growth trajectory remains strong despite the slowdown.
2. We adjusted our hiring trajectory to match revenue growth.

---

### 166. custom → bespoke

**Meaning:** made or designed specifically for a particular customer

**Examples:**
1. We offer bespoke software solutions for enterprise clients.
2. The consultancy delivered a bespoke strategy for the retailer.

---

### 167. customer → clientele

**Meaning:** the group of people who regularly buy from a business

**Examples:**
1. Our clientele has grown significantly since the rebrand.
2. The boutique caters to a loyal, high-value clientele.

---

### 168. cycle → iteration

**Meaning:** a repeated version of a process, each one improved

**Examples:**
1. Each product iteration incorporates feedback from the previous release.
2. The team completed three iterations before finalizing the design.

---

### 169. claim → assert

**Meaning:** to state something firmly as true

**Examples:**
1. The report asserts that customer satisfaction has improved significantly.
2. Management asserted that the delay would not affect the launch.

---

### 170. confident → assured

**Meaning:** feeling certain about one's abilities or outcomes

**Examples:**
1. The board seemed assured of the deal closing by year-end.
2. Leadership remains assured that targets will be met.

---

### 171. convey → articulate

**Meaning:** to express an idea or feeling clearly

**Examples:**
1. The presentation clearly articulated the company's five-year vision.
2. She articulated the team's concerns during the review meeting.

---

### 172. attain → secure

**Meaning:** to succeed in obtaining or achieving something

**Examples:**
1. The sales team secured a record number of new accounts this quarter.
2. We secured approval for the expanded budget.

---

### 173. average → median

**Meaning:** the middle value in a set of data

**Examples:**
1. The median salary across the department rose by three percent.
2. Analysts reported the median deal size increased this year.

---

### 174. blunt → forthright

**Meaning:** direct and honest, without holding back

**Examples:**
1. The consultant gave a forthright assessment of our weaknesses.
2. Her forthright feedback helped the team improve quickly.

---

### 175. bond → cultivate rapport

**Meaning:** to build a close, trusting relationship

**Examples:**
1. Account managers are trained to cultivate rapport with new clients.
2. Regular check-ins help cultivate rapport with remote teams.

---

### 176. bound → obligated

**Meaning:** required by duty or agreement to do something

**Examples:**
1. The vendor is obligated to replace any defective units.
2. Both parties are obligated to honor the terms of the contract.

---

### 177. box → constrain

**Meaning:** to limit or restrict something

**Examples:**
1. A tight budget constrained the scope of the marketing campaign.
2. Legacy infrastructure has constrained our ability to scale quickly.

---

### 178. brand → reputation

**Meaning:** the general opinion people hold about a company

**Examples:**
1. The company's reputation suffered after the data breach.
2. Strong customer service helped build the firm's reputation.

---

### 179. chase → pursue

**Meaning:** to follow or work toward something with effort

**Examples:**
1. The sales team will pursue three major leads this quarter.
2. We decided to pursue a partnership with the regional distributor.

---

### 180. above → exceeding

**Meaning:** greater than a stated amount or level

**Examples:**
1. Revenue came in exceeding the forecast by twelve percent.
2. Customer demand is exceeding our current production capacity.

---

## Section: D – F

### 181. do → execute

**Meaning:** to carry out a task or plan

**Examples:**
1. The team will execute the marketing plan by Friday.
2. Please execute the instructions outlined in the memo.

---

### 182. end → conclude

**Meaning:** to bring something to a close

**Examples:**
1. Let's conclude the meeting with a summary of action items.
2. The fiscal year will conclude on March 31.

---

### 183. fix → rectify

**Meaning:** to correct an error or problem

**Examples:**
1. We need to rectify the billing error before the invoice goes out.
2. The vendor promised to rectify the shipping delay.

---

### 184. find → identify

**Meaning:** to discover or pinpoint something

**Examples:**
1. The audit helped us identify several cost-saving opportunities.
2. Please identify the root cause of the outage.

---

### 185. fast → expedite

**Meaning:** to speed up a process

**Examples:**
1. Can you expedite the approval so we can launch on time?
2. We expedited shipping to meet the client's deadline.

---

### 186. easy → straightforward

**Meaning:** simple and uncomplicated

**Examples:**
1. The onboarding process is straightforward and takes ten minutes.
2. Integrating the new tool was straightforward for the IT team.

---

### 187. decide → determine

**Meaning:** to reach a conclusion after consideration

**Examples:**
1. Management will determine the budget allocation next week.
2. We need data to determine the best pricing strategy.

---

### 188. deal → address

**Meaning:** to handle or resolve an issue

**Examples:**
1. The support team will address the customer's complaint promptly.
2. We must address the staffing shortage before Q3.

---

### 189. discuss → deliberate

**Meaning:** to talk over carefully before deciding

**Examples:**
1. The board will deliberate on the merger proposal tomorrow.
2. Executives deliberated for hours before approving the budget.

---

### 190. full → comprehensive

**Meaning:** complete and including everything

**Examples:**
1. Please submit a comprehensive report by end of day.
2. The audit provided a comprehensive view of our finances.

---

### 191. first → initial

**Meaning:** happening at the beginning

**Examples:**
1. The initial findings suggest strong market demand.
2. Our initial proposal was well received by the client.

---

### 192. deliver → furnish

**Meaning:** to supply or provide something

**Examples:**
1. The vendor will furnish the equipment by next Monday.
2. We furnished the client with detailed financial statements.

---

### 193. feel → perceive

**Meaning:** to become aware of or interpret something

**Examples:**
1. Customers perceive the new pricing as more transparent.
2. Employees perceive the change as a positive step.

---

### 194. fair → equitable

**Meaning:** fair and impartial to all parties

**Examples:**
1. We aim for an equitable distribution of bonuses across teams.
2. The new policy ensures equitable treatment of all vendors.

---

### 195. few → limited

**Meaning:** a small amount or number

**Examples:**
1. We have a limited number of seats for the conference.
2. Limited resources forced us to prioritize key projects.

---

### 196. follow → adhere to

**Meaning:** to comply with rules or guidelines

**Examples:**
1. All contractors must adhere to the safety protocols.
2. The report adheres to the company's formatting standards.

---

### 197. force → compel

**Meaning:** to make someone do something through pressure

**Examples:**
1. Market pressure compelled us to lower our prices.
2. New regulations compel companies to disclose emissions data.

---

### 198. different → distinct

**Meaning:** clearly separate or unlike others

**Examples:**
1. Each region requires a distinct marketing strategy.
2. The two proposals offer distinct advantages.

---

### 199. difficult → challenging

**Meaning:** hard to deal with or accomplish

**Examples:**
1. This quarter presented a challenging economic environment.
2. Negotiating the contract terms proved challenging.

---

### 200. focus → prioritize

**Meaning:** to treat something as more important

**Examples:**
1. We need to prioritize customer retention this quarter.
2. The team prioritized the highest-impact projects.

---

### 201. form → constitute

**Meaning:** to make up or compose something

**Examples:**
1. These three departments constitute the new division.
2. Overseas sales now constitute 40% of revenue.

---

### 202. free → complimentary

**Meaning:** provided at no cost

**Examples:**
1. We offer a complimentary consultation to new clients.
2. The upgrade is complimentary for premium subscribers.

---

### 203. fresh → novel

**Meaning:** new and original

**Examples:**
1. The agency pitched a novel approach to the campaign.
2. Her novel idea reshaped the product roadmap.

---

### 204. fun → engaging

**Meaning:** interesting and enjoyable

**Examples:**
1. The workshop was engaging and well attended.
2. We designed an engaging onboarding experience for new hires.

---

### 205. fall → decline

**Meaning:** to decrease in amount or quality

**Examples:**
1. Revenue declined by 8% in the second quarter.
2. Customer satisfaction scores declined after the price increase.

---

### 206. finish → finalize

**Meaning:** to complete something fully

**Examples:**
1. Let's finalize the contract before the client call.
2. The design team will finalize the mockups tomorrow.

---

### 207. forget → overlook

**Meaning:** to fail to notice or consider

**Examples:**
1. Don't overlook the compliance requirements in the proposal.
2. We overlooked a key risk factor in the initial analysis.

---

### 208. fit → align

**Meaning:** to be consistent or in agreement with

**Examples:**
1. The new hire's skills align well with the role.
2. Our strategy aligns with the company's long-term goals.

---

### 209. describe → characterize

**Meaning:** to describe the nature of something

**Examples:**
1. Analysts characterize the market as highly volatile.
2. The report characterizes the risks in detail.

---

### 210. design → architect

**Meaning:** to plan and structure something carefully

**Examples:**
1. She architected the entire onboarding workflow.
2. The team architected a scalable data pipeline.

---

### 211. develop → cultivate

**Meaning:** to grow or improve something over time

**Examples:**
1. We aim to cultivate stronger relationships with suppliers.
2. The program cultivates leadership skills in junior staff.

---

### 212. decrease → diminish

**Meaning:** to become or make smaller

**Examples:**
1. Automation helped diminish operational costs.
2. Customer complaints have diminished since the update.

---

### 213. delay → defer

**Meaning:** to put off until a later time

**Examples:**
1. We had to defer the product launch to next quarter.
2. The committee deferred its decision pending more data.

---

### 214. expect → anticipate

**Meaning:** to expect something to happen

**Examples:**
1. We anticipate strong demand during the holiday season.
2. Analysts anticipate a rebound in Q4 earnings.

---

### 215. explain → elucidate

**Meaning:** to make something clear

**Examples:**
1. The presenter elucidated the new compliance policy.
2. This section elucidates our pricing methodology.

---

### 216. example → illustration

**Meaning:** something that shows or explains an idea

**Examples:**
1. The case study serves as a strong illustration of our approach.
2. Let me give an illustration of how the process works.

---

### 217. effort → endeavor

**Meaning:** a serious attempt to achieve something

**Examples:**
1. The merger was a joint endeavor between both firms.
2. This endeavor requires cross-departmental collaboration.

---

### 218. enter → initiate

**Meaning:** to begin something

**Examples:**
1. We plan to initiate talks with the potential investor.
2. The company initiated a new vendor onboarding process.

---

### 219. exact → precise

**Meaning:** accurate and specific

**Examples:**
1. We need precise figures before submitting the forecast.
2. Please provide a precise timeline for delivery.

---

### 220. extra → supplementary

**Meaning:** additional to what already exists

**Examples:**
1. We're offering a supplementary training session next week.
2. Supplementary funding was approved for the pilot program.

---

### 221. effect → impact

**Meaning:** a change or result caused by something

**Examples:**
1. The new policy had a positive impact on retention.
2. We measured the impact of the campaign on sales.

---

### 222. event → occurrence

**Meaning:** something that happens

**Examples:**
1. The system logs every occurrence of a failed login.
2. This was an isolated occurrence, not a trend.

---

### 223. equal → equivalent

**Meaning:** having the same value or status

**Examples:**
1. Both proposals offer roughly equivalent returns.
2. We offer equivalent benefits across all regions.

---

### 224. error → discrepancy

**Meaning:** a difference between what is expected and actual

**Examples:**
1. The audit uncovered a discrepancy in the quarterly report.
2. Please explain the discrepancy between the two figures.

---

### 225. earn → generate

**Meaning:** to produce or bring in something

**Examples:**
1. The campaign generated significant leads for the sales team.
2. The subsidiary generated $2M in revenue last year.

---

### 226. edit → revise

**Meaning:** to change or correct something

**Examples:**
1. Please revise the proposal before sending it to the client.
2. We revised the contract terms after legal review.

---

### 227. enough → sufficient

**Meaning:** as much as needed

**Examples:**
1. We have sufficient inventory to meet demand through Q4.
2. The budget was not sufficient to cover the expansion.

---

### 228. even → uniform

**Meaning:** consistent throughout

**Examples:**
1. We aim for a uniform experience across all branches.
2. The rollout ensured uniform pricing nationwide.

---

### 229. expand → broaden

**Meaning:** to make something larger in scope

**Examples:**
1. We plan to broaden our product line next year.
2. The firm broadened its client base into new markets.

---

### 230. extend → lengthen

**Meaning:** to make something longer in duration

**Examples:**
1. We agreed to lengthen the contract term by six months.
2. Management lengthened the trial period for new users.

---

### 231. explore → investigate

**Meaning:** to examine something carefully

**Examples:**
1. We're investigating new markets for expansion.
2. The team investigated alternative suppliers.

---

### 232. exist → persist

**Meaning:** to continue to be present

**Examples:**
1. The compatibility issue persists despite the update.
2. Concerns about pricing persist among customers.

---

### 233. encourage → incentivize

**Meaning:** to motivate someone to do something

**Examples:**
1. The bonus structure incentivizes higher performance.
2. We incentivize early adoption with a discount.

---

### 234. ensure → guarantee

**Meaning:** to make certain something happens

**Examples:**
1. We guarantee timely delivery for all premium orders.
2. This process guarantees data accuracy.

---

### 235. evaluate → assess

**Meaning:** to judge the value or quality of something

**Examples:**
1. HR will assess candidates based on core competencies.
2. We assessed the risks before signing the contract.

---

### 236. exchange → transact

**Meaning:** to conduct a business deal

**Examples:**
1. Clients can transact securely through our new portal.
2. We transacted the currency swap before markets closed.

---

### 237. expensive → exorbitant

**Meaning:** unreasonably high in cost

**Examples:**
1. The vendor's exorbitant fees forced us to renegotiate.
2. Clients complained the exorbitant pricing was unjustified.

---

### 238. empty → vacant

**Meaning:** containing nothing, unoccupied

**Examples:**
1. Several vacant positions remain unfilled in engineering.
2. The office space stayed vacant for six months.

---

### 239. eager → enthusiastic

**Meaning:** showing keen interest

**Examples:**
1. The new hires were enthusiastic about the onboarding program.
2. Investors seemed enthusiastic about the growth strategy.

---

### 240. excite → galvanize

**Meaning:** to inspire into action

**Examples:**
1. The keynote galvanized the sales team ahead of launch.
2. Her speech galvanized employees to embrace the change.

---

### 241. elect → designate

**Meaning:** to officially choose someone for a role

**Examples:**
1. The board designated her as interim CEO.
2. We designated a lead for each workstream.

---

### 242. elevate → enhance

**Meaning:** to improve the quality of something

**Examples:**
1. The redesign enhances the overall user experience.
2. New training enhances employee performance.

---

### 243. essential → imperative

**Meaning:** extremely important, necessary

**Examples:**
1. It is imperative that we meet the compliance deadline.
2. Clear communication is imperative during a crisis.

---

### 244. equip → outfit

**Meaning:** to provide with necessary tools

**Examples:**
1. We outfitted the sales team with new CRM software.
2. Each branch was outfitted with updated hardware.

---

### 245. establish → institute

**Meaning:** to set up or introduce something formally

**Examples:**
1. The company instituted a new remote work policy.
2. We instituted stricter approval processes after the audit.

---

### 246. estimate → project

**Meaning:** to forecast a future value

**Examples:**
1. We project revenue growth of 12% next year.
2. Analysts project a slowdown in the second half.

---

### 247. evolve → transform

**Meaning:** to change significantly over time

**Examples:**
1. The company transformed its business model post-pandemic.
2. Digital tools are transforming how we serve clients.

---

### 248. examine → scrutinize

**Meaning:** to inspect something closely

**Examples:**
1. Regulators scrutinized the merger for antitrust concerns.
2. The finance team scrutinized every line item in the budget.

---

### 249. exceed → surpass

**Meaning:** to go beyond a target or limit

**Examples:**
1. Sales surpassed expectations in the third quarter.
2. Our savings surpassed the projected target.

---

### 250. excuse → justification

**Meaning:** a reason given to explain something

**Examples:**
1. The vendor offered no justification for the delay.
2. We need a clear justification for the budget overrun.

---

### 251. exit → withdraw

**Meaning:** to leave or remove oneself from something

**Examples:**
1. The investor decided to withdraw from the partnership.
2. We withdrew our bid after reviewing the terms.

---

### 252. expose → unveil

**Meaning:** to reveal something publicly

**Examples:**
1. The company will unveil its new product line next month.
2. We unveiled the rebranding strategy at the town hall.

---

### 253. edge → advantage

**Meaning:** a favorable position over competitors

**Examples:**
1. Our proprietary technology gives us a competitive advantage.
2. Early adoption gave the firm a market advantage.

---

### 254. early → preliminary

**Meaning:** happening before the main event

**Examples:**
1. Preliminary results suggest strong customer interest.
2. We shared preliminary findings with the board.

---

### 255. eye → monitor

**Meaning:** to watch closely over time

**Examples:**
1. We monitor competitor pricing on a weekly basis.
2. The team monitors key performance indicators daily.

---

### 256. employ → utilize

**Meaning:** to make use of something

**Examples:**
1. We utilize automation to reduce manual errors.
2. The team utilized customer feedback to refine the product.

---

### 257. enjoy → relish

**Meaning:** to take great pleasure in something

**Examples:**
1. The team relished the challenge of the new market.
2. She relishes opportunities to mentor junior staff.

---

### 258. engage → captivate

**Meaning:** to hold someone's attention strongly

**Examples:**
1. The presentation captivated the entire boardroom.
2. Our campaign captivated a younger audience.

---

### 259. enable → facilitate

**Meaning:** to make something easier to happen

**Examples:**
1. The new platform facilitates faster invoice processing.
2. This tool facilitates collaboration across time zones.

---

### 260. erase → expunge

**Meaning:** to completely remove something

**Examples:**
1. Outdated records will be expunged from the database.
2. We expunged duplicate entries during the data cleanup.

---

### 261. eat → erode

**Meaning:** to gradually wear away or reduce

**Examples:**
1. Rising costs are eroding our profit margins.
2. Inflation eroded consumer purchasing power this year.

---

### 262. detail → elaborate on

**Meaning:** to explain something in more depth

**Examples:**
1. Please elaborate on the risks outlined in section three.
2. The consultant elaborated on the implementation timeline.

---

### 263. doubt → skepticism

**Meaning:** a feeling of uncertainty about something

**Examples:**
1. Investors expressed skepticism about the growth projections.
2. There was some skepticism around the new vendor's reliability.

---

### 264. drop → discontinue

**Meaning:** to stop producing or offering something

**Examples:**
1. We decided to discontinue the underperforming product line.
2. The airline discontinued the route due to low demand.

---

### 265. drive → spearhead

**Meaning:** to lead an initiative or effort

**Examples:**
1. She will spearhead the digital transformation project.
2. The CTO spearheaded the migration to the cloud.

---

### 266. draft → formulate

**Meaning:** to create a plan or document carefully

**Examples:**
1. Legal will formulate the terms of the new agreement.
2. We formulated a strategy to enter the Asian market.

---

### 267. direct → orchestrate

**Meaning:** to organize and coordinate carefully

**Examples:**
1. The project manager orchestrated the product launch.
2. She orchestrated the cross-functional rollout.

---

### 268. divide → segment

**Meaning:** to split into distinct parts

**Examples:**
1. We segmented customers by purchasing behavior.
2. The market was segmented into three regions.

---

### 269. deep → profound

**Meaning:** very great or intense

**Examples:**
1. The merger had a profound impact on company culture.
2. Leadership has a profound understanding of the industry.

---

### 270. down → declining

**Meaning:** decreasing in value or performance

**Examples:**
1. Engagement metrics have been declining since the redesign.
2. We addressed the declining conversion rate immediately.

---

### 271. redo → revamp

**Meaning:** to change something substantially to improve it

**Examples:**
1. We revamped the onboarding process based on feedback.
2. The website was revamped to improve usability.

---

### 272. define → delineate

**Meaning:** to describe the exact nature of something

**Examples:**
1. The contract delineates each party's responsibilities.
2. We need to delineate the project scope clearly.

---

### 273. depend → rely

**Meaning:** to need something for support

**Examples:**
1. The forecast relies heavily on stable commodity prices.
2. Our supply chain relies on a single key vendor.

---

### 274. damage → compromise

**Meaning:** to weaken or harm something

**Examples:**
1. The outage compromised customer trust in our platform.
2. A data breach could compromise our reputation.

---

### 275. dangerous → hazardous

**Meaning:** risky or likely to cause harm

**Examples:**
1. The audit flagged several hazardous compliance gaps.
2. Overleveraging can be hazardous to long-term stability.

---

### 276. demand → require

**Meaning:** to need something as essential

**Examples:**
1. The role requires strong analytical skills.
2. Regulators require quarterly disclosure of emissions data.

---

### 277. deny → refute

**Meaning:** to prove something is false

**Examples:**
1. The company refuted claims of misleading advertising.
2. Management refuted the rumors of an imminent layoff.

---

### 278. desire → aspire to

**Meaning:** to hope to achieve something

**Examples:**
1. We aspire to become the market leader within five years.
2. The startup aspires to a nationwide rollout by 2027.

---

### 279. destroy → eliminate

**Meaning:** to remove something completely

**Examples:**
1. Automation eliminated several redundant manual steps.
2. The merger eliminated overlapping departments.

---

### 280. die → lapse

**Meaning:** to come to an end, often unintentionally

**Examples:**
1. The partnership lapsed after both parties lost interest.
2. The offer will lapse if not accepted by Friday.

---

### 281. dim → obscure

**Meaning:** unclear or difficult to understand

**Examples:**
1. The vague wording obscured the true cost of the contract.
2. Complex jargon obscured the report's key findings.

---

### 282. dirty → corrupted

**Meaning:** containing errors or flaws

**Examples:**
1. The migration failed due to corrupted source data.
2. We cleaned the corrupted dataset before analysis.

---

### 283. disagree → dissent

**Meaning:** to hold a different opinion

**Examples:**
1. Two board members dissented from the majority decision.
2. Employees were free to dissent during the review.

---

### 284. discover → ascertain

**Meaning:** to find out something through investigation

**Examples:**
1. We ascertained the cause of the delay through the audit.
2. The team ascertained that demand had shifted regionally.

---

### 285. dismiss → disregard

**Meaning:** to decide not to consider something

**Examples:**
1. We shouldn't disregard the early warning signs.
2. The proposal disregarded key budget constraints.

---

### 286. display → exhibit

**Meaning:** to show something publicly

**Examples:**
1. The dashboard exhibits real-time performance metrics.
2. The team exhibited strong resilience during the crisis.

---

### 287. dispose → divest

**Meaning:** to get rid of an asset or business

**Examples:**
1. The company divested its underperforming retail division.
2. We plan to divest non-core assets by year-end.

---

### 288. distribute → disseminate

**Meaning:** to spread information or resources widely

**Examples:**
1. Please disseminate the policy update to all staff.
2. We disseminated the survey results across departments.

---

### 289. disturb → disrupt

**Meaning:** to interrupt or interfere with something

**Examples:**
1. The outage disrupted operations for several hours.
2. New technology continues to disrupt traditional retail.

---

### 290. double → duplicate

**Meaning:** to make an exact copy or match

**Examples:**
1. We aim to duplicate last year's record sales figures.
2. The error caused the system to duplicate every invoice.

---

### 291. drag → prolong

**Meaning:** to make something last longer than needed

**Examples:**
1. Lengthy approvals prolonged the procurement process.
2. Legal disputes prolonged the merger by several months.

---

### 292. draw → infer

**Meaning:** to reach a conclusion from evidence

**Examples:**
1. We can infer strong demand from the pre-order numbers.
2. Analysts inferred a slowdown from the latest data.

---

### 293. dream → aspiration

**Meaning:** a strong hope or ambition

**Examples:**
1. Expanding internationally remains a key aspiration for the founders.
2. The company's aspiration is to lead in sustainable manufacturing.

---

### 294. duty → obligation

**Meaning:** something one is required to do

**Examples:**
1. Vendors have a contractual obligation to meet delivery deadlines.
2. We take our obligation to shareholders seriously.

---

### 295. dumb → impractical

**Meaning:** not sensible or realistic

**Examples:**
1. The proposed timeline proved impractical given our resources.
2. Leadership dismissed the plan as impractical.

---

### 296. done → settled

**Meaning:** agreed upon and fully resolved

**Examples:**
1. The contract terms are settled and ready for signature.
2. Once the budget is settled, we can proceed.

---

### 297. daring → audacious

**Meaning:** bold and willing to take risks

**Examples:**
1. The startup pursued an audacious expansion into new markets.
2. Investors were impressed by the audacious growth targets.

---

### 298. dip → downturn

**Meaning:** a period of decline

**Examples:**
1. Sales experienced a slight downturn during the recession.
2. The industry is recovering from last year's downturn.

---

### 299. drift → digress

**Meaning:** to move away from the main topic

**Examples:**
1. Let's not digress from today's agenda items.
2. The discussion digressed into unrelated budget concerns.

---

### 300. figure out → deduce

**Meaning:** to work something out through reasoning

**Examples:**
1. We deduced the cause of the revenue drop from the sales data.
2. The consultant deduced a pattern in customer churn.

---

### 301. fear → apprehension

**Meaning:** a feeling of worry about something

**Examples:**
1. There is some apprehension among staff about the restructuring.
2. Investors showed apprehension ahead of the earnings call.

---

### 302. flexible → adaptable

**Meaning:** able to adjust easily to change

**Examples:**
1. Our workforce needs to be adaptable to remote work policies.
2. The platform is adaptable to a variety of business needs.

---

### 303. flow → cascade

**Meaning:** to pass down or spread through stages

**Examples:**
1. Strategic goals cascade down to individual team targets.
2. Changes to pricing cascade through the entire supply chain.

---

### 304. fund → finance

**Meaning:** to provide money for something

**Examples:**
1. The bank agreed to finance the new facility.
2. Venture capital financed the startup's expansion.

---

### 305. fault → deficiency

**Meaning:** a shortcoming or weakness

**Examples:**
1. The audit identified a deficiency in our internal controls.
2. We addressed the deficiency before the compliance review.

---

### 306. fake → fraudulent

**Meaning:** dishonest or deceptive

**Examples:**
1. The company was fined for fraudulent accounting practices.
2. We flagged several fraudulent transactions this month.

---

### 307. flaw → imperfection

**Meaning:** a minor defect

**Examples:**
1. Quality control caught a minor imperfection before shipping.
2. The report noted an imperfection in the testing process.

---

### 308. flood → saturate

**Meaning:** to fill excessively

**Examples:**
1. Competitors saturated the market with low-cost alternatives.
2. The inbox was saturated with customer inquiries after launch.

---

### 309. fluctuate → oscillate

**Meaning:** to move back and forth irregularly

**Examples:**
1. Exchange rates oscillated throughout the quarter.
2. Demand oscillated due to seasonal trends.

---

### 310. follow up → pursue

**Meaning:** to continue working toward something

**Examples:**
1. Sales will pursue the lead after the initial demo.
2. We pursued the partnership opportunity for months.

---

### 311. forward → transmit

**Meaning:** to send something to another location

**Examples:**
1. Please transmit the signed contract to legal.
2. The report was transmitted to all regional managers.

---

### 312. foster → nurture

**Meaning:** to encourage the growth or development of something

**Examples:**
1. The mentorship program nurtures leadership talent.
2. We nurture long-term relationships with key clients.

---

### 313. found → inaugurate

**Meaning:** to formally begin or establish something

**Examples:**
1. The company will inaugurate its new headquarters next month.
2. They inaugurated the innovation lab last spring.

---

### 314. free up → liberate

**Meaning:** to make something available by removing constraints

**Examples:**
1. Automating invoicing liberated hours of manual work each week.
2. Cutting overhead liberated funds for R&D.

---

### 315. freeze → suspend

**Meaning:** to temporarily stop something

**Examples:**
1. The company suspended hiring during the review period.
2. We suspended the project pending budget approval.

---

### 316. frequent → recurrent

**Meaning:** happening often, repeated

**Examples:**
1. Recurrent system errors delayed several client deliveries.
2. Recurrent complaints prompted a review of the process.

---

### 317. friction → discord

**Meaning:** disagreement or tension between people

**Examples:**
1. Poor communication created discord between departments.
2. The merger caused some discord among leadership teams.

---

### 318. fill → fulfill

**Meaning:** to complete or satisfy a requirement

**Examples:**
1. We fulfilled the order ahead of the promised deadline.
2. The vendor fulfilled every term of the contract.

---

### 319. funny → anomalous

**Meaning:** unusual or irregular

**Examples:**
1. The finance team flagged anomalous activity in the accounts.
2. An anomalous spike in traffic triggered an investigation.

---

### 320. fuse → consolidate

**Meaning:** to combine into a single unified whole

**Examples:**
1. The two departments were consolidated to cut costs.
2. We consolidated our vendors to simplify procurement.

---

### 321. fuel → propel

**Meaning:** to drive forward or energize

**Examples:**
1. Strong demand propelled revenue growth this year.
2. Innovation continues to propel the company's expansion.

---

### 322. fight → contest

**Meaning:** to formally challenge or dispute something

**Examples:**
1. The vendor contested the penalty clause in the contract.
2. We plan to contest the regulator's decision.

---

### 323. flat → stagnant

**Meaning:** not growing or changing

**Examples:**
1. Revenue remained stagnant throughout the third quarter.
2. The stagnant market forced us to rethink our strategy.

---

### 324. flee → retreat

**Meaning:** to withdraw from a difficult situation

**Examples:**
1. Investors retreated from the sector amid volatility.
2. The company retreated from the international market after losses.

---

### 325. follow through → consummate

**Meaning:** to complete an agreement fully

**Examples:**
1. Both parties consummated the deal after final due diligence.
2. The acquisition was consummated in early January.

---

### 326. faith → confidence

**Meaning:** trust in something's reliability

**Examples:**
1. Shareholders expressed confidence in the new leadership.
2. Clients have confidence in our long-term strategy.

---

### 327. favor → endorse

**Meaning:** to publicly support something

**Examples:**
1. The board endorsed the proposed merger.
2. Analysts endorsed the company's turnaround strategy.

---

### 328. feed → supply

**Meaning:** to provide something continuously

**Examples:**
1. The factory supplies components to three assembly plants.
2. Reliable data supplies our forecasting models.

---

### 329. file → submit

**Meaning:** to formally send in a document

**Examples:**
1. Please submit the expense report by Friday.
2. We submitted the compliance documents ahead of the deadline.

---

### 330. firm → resolute

**Meaning:** showing determination, not changing one's position

**Examples:**
1. Leadership remained resolute despite market pressure.
2. The negotiating team stayed resolute on pricing terms.

---

## Section: G – K

### 331. get → obtain

**Meaning:** to acquire or receive something, often through effort or a formal process

**Examples:**
1. We need to obtain sign-off from legal before the contract is sent.
2. Please obtain the client's approval before finalizing the design.

---

### 332. give → provide

**Meaning:** to supply or make something available to someone

**Examples:**
1. The vendor will provide a detailed cost breakdown by Friday.
2. Our team can provide additional support during the product launch.

---

### 333. good → exceptional

**Meaning:** outstanding in quality, far above the average

**Examples:**
1. The sales team delivered exceptional results this quarter.
2. Her exceptional presentation impressed the entire board.

---

### 334. go → proceed

**Meaning:** to move forward with a plan or action

**Examples:**
1. We will proceed with the merger once due diligence is complete.
2. The project manager confirmed we can proceed to the next phase.

---

### 335. help → facilitate

**Meaning:** to make a process easier by supporting or guiding it

**Examples:**
1. The consultant will facilitate the workshop on Tuesday.
2. This new software will facilitate faster invoice processing.

---

### 336. important → critical

**Meaning:** essential or of vital significance to an outcome

**Examples:**
1. Meeting the compliance deadline is critical to avoiding penalties.
2. Customer retention is a critical priority for next year's strategy.

---

### 337. increase → augment

**Meaning:** to make something greater in size, amount, or degree

**Examples:**
1. The firm plans to augment its workforce ahead of the busy season.
2. We will augment our marketing budget to support the new launch.

---

### 338. improve → enhance

**Meaning:** to make something better in quality or value

**Examples:**
1. The update will enhance the user experience significantly.
2. Training sessions are designed to enhance employee productivity.

---

### 339. group → cluster

**Meaning:** a set of related items or people gathered together

**Examples:**
1. We identified a cluster of customers with similar buying habits.
2. The analysts organized the survey responses into three clusters.

---

### 340. include → incorporate

**Meaning:** to add something as part of a larger whole

**Examples:**
1. The final report will incorporate feedback from all stakeholders.
2. We plan to incorporate the new safety guidelines into the manual.

---

### 341. high → elevated

**Meaning:** notably above normal or expected levels

**Examples:**
1. The company reported elevated demand for its subscription service.
2. Elevated shipping costs affected our margins this quarter.

---

### 342. hard → challenging

**Meaning:** difficult to accomplish or deal with

**Examples:**
1. Entering the Asian market proved challenging due to regulations.
2. The team faced a challenging deadline for the product rollout.

---

### 343. information → insights

**Meaning:** valuable knowledge or understanding drawn from data

**Examples:**
1. The dashboard provides real-time insights into customer behavior.
2. Our analysts shared key insights from the market research.

---

### 344. issue → matter

**Meaning:** a subject or problem that requires attention

**Examples:**
1. This is a sensitive matter that requires the CEO's input.
2. We need to address the staffing matter before the fiscal year ends.

---

### 345. idea → concept

**Meaning:** a general notion or plan for something

**Examples:**
1. The design team presented a bold new concept for the packaging.
2. We are still refining the concept before pitching investors.

---

### 346. happen → occur

**Meaning:** to take place or come about

**Examples:**
1. Delays in shipping occurred due to the port closure.
2. A significant policy change is expected to occur next quarter.

---

### 347. keep → retain

**Meaning:** to continue to have or hold something

**Examples:**
1. The company aims to retain top talent through better benefits.
2. We must retain client trust even during periods of change.

---

### 348. know → comprehend

**Meaning:** to fully understand something

**Examples:**
1. New hires must comprehend the compliance policy before starting.
2. It's essential that stakeholders comprehend the risks involved.

---

### 349. job → role

**Meaning:** a specific position or set of responsibilities within an organization

**Examples:**
1. She was promoted to a senior role in the finance division.
2. The recruiter is filling a newly created role on the product team.

---

### 350. have → possess

**Meaning:** to hold or own something, often a quality or resource

**Examples:**
1. The candidate must possess strong analytical skills.
2. Our firm possesses the expertise needed for this acquisition.

---

### 351. join → merge

**Meaning:** to combine two or more things into one

**Examples:**
1. The two departments will merge to reduce operational overlap.
2. Our company plans to merge with a regional competitor next year.

---

### 352. hire → recruit

**Meaning:** to bring someone on to work for an organization

**Examples:**
1. HR plans to recruit five new analysts this quarter.
2. We are recruiting for a data science role in Bangalore.

---

### 353. key → pivotal

**Meaning:** extremely important, central to success

**Examples:**
1. Customer feedback played a pivotal role in the redesign.
2. This partnership is pivotal to our expansion strategy.

---

### 354. goal → objective

**Meaning:** a specific result an organization aims to achieve

**Examples:**
1. Our primary objective this year is to increase market share.
2. The team set clear objectives for the product launch.

---

### 355. gain → acquire

**Meaning:** to obtain something, often through effort or investment

**Examples:**
1. The company plans to acquire a smaller competitor next year.
2. We aim to acquire new customers through targeted campaigns.

---

### 356. guide → steer

**Meaning:** to direct the course or progress of something

**Examples:**
1. The board will steer the company through this transition period.
2. Her experience helped steer the negotiations toward a fair deal.

---

### 357. growth → expansion

**Meaning:** the process of becoming larger or more developed

**Examples:**
1. The company is planning an aggressive expansion into new markets.
2. Investors are pleased with the steady expansion of revenue.

---

### 358. hold → maintain

**Meaning:** to keep something at a steady or consistent level

**Examples:**
1. We must maintain quality standards as we scale production.
2. The firm managed to maintain profitability despite the downturn.

---

### 359. involve → entail

**Meaning:** to include or require something as a necessary part

**Examples:**
1. The new role will entail frequent travel to client sites.
2. Rolling out the software entails extensive staff training.

---

### 360. impact → ramifications

**Meaning:** the wider consequences or effects of an action

**Examples:**
1. The policy change has significant ramifications for our supply chain.
2. Leadership discussed the ramifications of delaying the launch.

---

### 361. gather → compile

**Meaning:** to collect information or items into a single set

**Examples:**
1. The team will compile customer survey data by Monday.
2. Please compile all invoices before the audit begins.

---

### 362. handle → manage

**Meaning:** to deal with or take charge of a task or situation

**Examples:**
1. She will manage the client account going forward.
2. Our support team manages over a thousand tickets weekly.

---

### 363. huge → substantial

**Meaning:** very large in size, amount, or degree
