---

### 696. polite → courteous

**Meaning:** showing good manners and respect toward others

**Examples:**
1. Our staff are trained to remain courteous under pressure.
2. The client appreciated our courteous handling of the complaint.

---

### 697. poll → survey

**Meaning:** a method of gathering opinions from a group of people

**Examples:**
1. We conducted a survey to gauge employee satisfaction.
2. The customer survey revealed strong demand for the feature.

---

### 698. portion → allocation

**Meaning:** a designated share of a larger amount

**Examples:**
1. A significant allocation of the budget went to R&D.
2. Each department receives an annual allocation.

---

### 699. position → stance

**Meaning:** an official opinion or approach taken on an issue

**Examples:**
1. The company clarified its stance on remote work.
2. Our stance on pricing remains firm for now.

---

### 700. possess → command

**Meaning:** to have or control something of value

**Examples:**
1. The firm commands significant market share in Asia.
2. She commands deep expertise in supply chain management.

---

### 701. potential → prospective

**Meaning:** likely to become something in the future

**Examples:**
1. We reached out to several prospective clients this week.
2. The recruiter identified three prospective candidates.

---

### 702. powerful → formidable

**Meaning:** strong and impressive, often hard to compete against

**Examples:**
1. We now face a formidable competitor in this segment.
2. The merger created a formidable presence in the market.

---

### 703. practical → pragmatic

**Meaning:** focused on sensible, workable solutions

**Examples:**
1. The manager took a pragmatic approach to budget cuts.
2. We need a pragmatic solution given the tight deadline.

---

### 704. predict → forecast

**Meaning:** to estimate what will happen in the future

**Examples:**
1. Analysts forecast steady growth for the next fiscal year.
2. We forecast a rise in demand during the holidays.

---

### 705. prefer → favor

**Meaning:** to like one option more than another

**Examples:**
1. Investors favor companies with strong cash flow.
2. The committee favored the vendor with the fastest delivery.

---

### 706. preliminary → tentative

**Meaning:** not yet final, subject to change

**Examples:**
1. We reached a tentative agreement on pricing terms.
2. These are tentative figures pending final review.

---

### 707. premise → assumption

**Meaning:** an idea taken to be true as a basis for reasoning

**Examples:**
1. Our forecast is based on the assumption of stable demand.
2. We should challenge that assumption before finalizing the plan.

---

### 708. preserve → maintain

**Meaning:** to keep something in its current, good condition

**Examples:**
1. We aim to maintain our reputation for quality service.
2. The team worked to maintain client relationships during the transition.

---

### 709. press → urge

**Meaning:** to strongly encourage someone to take action

**Examples:**
1. The board urged management to cut costs immediately.
2. We urged the supplier to expedite the shipment.

---

### 710. pressure → constraint

**Meaning:** a limiting factor that restricts options

**Examples:**
1. Budget constraints delayed the product launch.
2. We are operating under significant time constraints.

---

### 711. pretend → simulate

**Meaning:** to create a realistic imitation of a situation for testing

**Examples:**
1. We simulated a system outage to test our response plan.
2. The training program simulates real client negotiations.

---

### 712. previous → prior

**Meaning:** coming before the current one

**Examples:**
1. Revenue exceeded the prior quarter's results.
2. We reviewed the prior report before making changes.

---

### 713. pride → distinction

**Meaning:** a quality that sets something apart as excellent

**Examples:**
1. The firm handles every project with distinction.
2. Our team takes pride in delivering work of distinction.

---

### 714. primary → principal

**Meaning:** the main or most important one

**Examples:**
1. Cost reduction is our principal objective this quarter.
2. The principal concern raised was data security.

---

### 715. print → publish

**Meaning:** to make content officially available to the public

**Examples:**
1. We will publish the annual report next week.
2. The findings were published in the industry journal.

---

### 716. prior → preceding

**Meaning:** coming before, especially in sequence or importance

**Examples:**
1. This step requires preceding sign-off from finance.
2. The preceding phase must be completed before launch.

---

### 717. prize → accolade

**Meaning:** an award recognizing excellence or achievement

**Examples:**
1. The firm received an industry accolade for innovation.
2. Her team earned several accolades this year.

---

### 718. probable → anticipated

**Meaning:** expected to happen based on current information

**Examples:**
1. We anticipate a rise in raw material costs next quarter.
2. An anticipated delay pushed the launch to October.

---

### 719. procedure → mechanism

**Meaning:** a system or method by which something is done

**Examples:**
1. We introduced a new mechanism for tracking expenses.
2. The escalation mechanism ensures issues are resolved quickly.

---

### 720. proceed → advance

**Meaning:** to move forward with a plan or process

**Examples:**
1. We will advance to the next phase pending approval.
2. The deal is set to advance after due diligence.

---

### 721. produce → generate

**Meaning:** to create or bring about a result

**Examples:**
1. The campaign generated a 20% increase in leads.
2. This initiative is expected to generate significant revenue.

---

### 722. profit → earnings

**Meaning:** the money a company earns after expenses

**Examples:**
1. Quarterly earnings exceeded analyst expectations.
2. The company reported strong earnings despite inflation.

---

### 723. program → initiative

**Meaning:** a planned series of actions to achieve a goal

**Examples:**
1. The wellness initiative improved employee retention.
2. We launched a new sustainability initiative this year.

---

### 724. prohibit → restrict

**Meaning:** to limit or forbid a particular action

**Examples:**
1. Company policy restricts personal use of corporate devices.
2. The new regulation restricts data sharing with third parties.

---

### 725. prominent → distinguished

**Meaning:** well-known and highly respected

**Examples:**
1. We hired a distinguished expert to lead the initiative.
2. The firm holds a distinguished position in the industry.

---

### 726. proof → evidence

**Meaning:** information that supports a claim or conclusion

**Examples:**
1. The audit provided evidence of consistent cost overruns.
2. We need evidence to support the proposed changes.

---

### 727. property → asset

**Meaning:** something of value owned by a person or company

**Examples:**
1. Intellectual property is one of our most valuable assets.
2. The firm acquired several assets during the merger.

---

### 728. proportion → ratio

**Meaning:** the relative amount of one thing compared to another

**Examples:**
1. We track the ratio of support tickets to active users.
2. The debt-to-equity ratio improved this quarter.

---

### 729. proposal → pitch

**Meaning:** a plan presented to persuade someone to accept it

**Examples:**
1. We delivered a compelling pitch to the investment committee.
2. The sales team refined its pitch based on client feedback.

---

### 730. prospect → lead

**Meaning:** a potential customer or opportunity

**Examples:**
1. The team followed up with every qualified lead this week.
2. We generated fifty new leads from the trade show.

---

### 731. protest → objection

**Meaning:** a formal statement of disagreement

**Examples:**
1. Legal filed an objection to the proposed contract terms.
2. The union raised an objection to the new schedule.

---

### 732. proud → gratified

**Meaning:** feeling satisfaction from an achievement

**Examples:**
1. We are gratified by the positive response to our launch.
2. Leadership was gratified with the record quarterly results.

---

### 733. provoke → trigger

**Meaning:** to cause a reaction or response

**Examples:**
1. The price increase triggered a wave of customer complaints.
2. The announcement triggered a spike in stock price.

---

### 734. publish → disseminate

**Meaning:** to distribute information widely

**Examples:**
1. We disseminated the policy update to all employees.
2. The report will be disseminated to stakeholders next week.

---

### 735. pull → withdraw

**Meaning:** to remove something from use or consideration

**Examples:**
1. The company withdrew the product due to safety concerns.
2. Investors withdrew funding amid market uncertainty.

---

### 736. purchase → procure

**Meaning:** to obtain something, often through a formal process

**Examples:**
1. Procurement will procure new equipment for the facility.
2. We procured raw materials from a certified supplier.

---

### 737. pure → genuine

**Meaning:** sincere and authentic, without pretense

**Examples:**
1. The CEO expressed genuine concern for employee wellbeing.
2. Clients value our genuine commitment to service quality.

---

### 738. puzzle → conundrum

**Meaning:** a difficult problem that is hard to solve

**Examples:**
1. Pricing strategy remains a conundrum for the leadership team.
2. Talent retention is a conundrum across the industry.

---

### 739. quality → caliber

**Meaning:** the standard of excellence of something

**Examples:**
1. We hired candidates of exceptional caliber this year.
2. The caliber of our service sets us apart from competitors.

---

### 740. quantity → volume

**Meaning:** the amount of something produced or available

**Examples:**
1. Production volume increased by fifteen percent.
2. We negotiated a discount based on order volume.

---

### 741. question → inquiry

**Meaning:** a formal request for information

**Examples:**
1. We received several inquiries about the new pricing plan.
2. Please direct any inquiries to the support team.

---

### 742. quick → swift

**Meaning:** happening or done with speed

**Examples:**
1. We appreciate the vendor's swift response to the issue.
2. A swift resolution helped retain the client's trust.

---

### 743. quiet → discreet

**Meaning:** careful not to attract attention, especially with sensitive matters

**Examples:**
1. The negotiations were kept discreet until finalized.
2. HR handled the matter in a discreet manner.

---

### 744. quit → resign

**Meaning:** to formally leave a job or position

**Examples:**
1. She resigned from her position after five years.
2. Two senior managers resigned last quarter.

---

### 745. quote → cite

**Meaning:** to reference a source or figure as evidence

**Examples:**
1. The report cites industry data to support its conclusions.
2. Please cite the source when using external statistics.

---

### 746. raise → elevate

**Meaning:** to increase the level or amount of something

**Examples:**
1. The company elevated its customer service standards.
2. We aim to elevate brand awareness through this campaign.

---

### 747. random → arbitrary

**Meaning:** not based on any particular reason or system

**Examples:**
1. The decision seemed arbitrary without supporting data.
2. We avoided arbitrary cuts by reviewing each budget line.

---

### 748. range → spectrum

**Meaning:** the full variety of options or types available

**Examples:**
1. Our product spans a wide spectrum of price points.
2. The team includes talent from across the skill spectrum.

---

### 749. rank → prioritize

**Meaning:** to arrange items in order of importance

**Examples:**
1. We prioritized features based on customer demand.
2. The team prioritized high-value accounts this quarter.

---

### 750. rapid → brisk

**Meaning:** happening quickly, especially in business activity

**Examples:**
1. The market saw brisk demand for the new product.
2. Sales grew at a brisk pace throughout the summer.

---

### 751. rare → scarce

**Meaning:** not common; in short supply

**Examples:**
1. Skilled technicians are scarce in this region.
2. The scarce inventory drove prices higher.

---

### 752. rate → pace

**Meaning:** the speed at which something happens or changes

**Examples:**
1. The company is expanding at an impressive pace.
2. We need to increase the pace of hiring.

---

### 753. raw → unprocessed

**Meaning:** in its original, unrefined state

**Examples:**
1. The report includes unprocessed survey data for review.
2. We source unprocessed materials directly from suppliers.

---

### 754. reach → attain

**Meaning:** to successfully achieve a goal or level

**Examples:**
1. The team attained its sales target ahead of schedule.
2. We aim to attain full compliance by year-end.

---

### 755. react → respond

**Meaning:** to act in reply to something that has happened

**Examples:**
1. The company responded quickly to the negative press.
2. We must respond to customer feedback within 24 hours.

---

### 756. ready → prepared

**Meaning:** fully equipped and organized for what comes next

**Examples:**
1. The team is prepared for tomorrow's client presentation.
2. Ensure the warehouse is prepared for the holiday surge.

---

### 757. real → authentic

**Meaning:** true and not fabricated or exaggerated

**Examples:**
1. Customers respond well to authentic brand storytelling.
2. We seek authentic feedback from every client survey.

---

### 758. realize → ascertain

**Meaning:** to discover or establish something as a fact

**Examples:**
1. The audit helped us ascertain the source of the discrepancy.
2. We ascertained that the delay was caused by a supplier issue.

---

### 759. reason → justification

**Meaning:** an explanation given to support a decision or action

**Examples:**
1. Please provide justification for the additional headcount.
2. The justification for the price increase was rising costs.

---

### 760. receive → obtain

**Meaning:** to get or acquire something

**Examples:**
1. We obtained approval from legal before proceeding.
2. The team obtained the necessary certifications this year.

---

### 761. recent → prevailing

**Meaning:** existing or happening at the present time

**Examples:**
1. The prevailing market conditions favor a cautious approach.
2. We adjusted our strategy to reflect prevailing customer preferences.

---

### 762. recognize → acknowledge

**Meaning:** to formally accept or admit that something is true

**Examples:**
1. Management acknowledged the delay and issued an apology.
2. We acknowledge the challenges raised in the feedback survey.

---

### 763. recommend → advocate

**Meaning:** to publicly support a particular course of action

**Examples:**
1. The consultant advocated for a phased rollout.
2. We advocate adopting the new compliance framework.

---

### 764. record → document

**Meaning:** to officially note or register information

**Examples:**
1. Please document all client interactions in the CRM.
2. The incident was documented for future reference.

---

### 765. recover → rebound

**Meaning:** to return to a normal or successful state after a setback

**Examples:**
1. The market is expected to rebound by next quarter.
2. Sales rebounded after the promotional campaign launched.

---

### 766. reduce → curtail

**Meaning:** to cut back or limit something

**Examples:**
1. The company curtailed travel expenses to manage costs.
2. We curtailed spending on non-essential projects.

---

### 767. refer → route

**Meaning:** to send something or someone to the appropriate place

**Examples:**
1. Complex cases are routed to the senior support team.
2. Please route this inquiry to the finance department.

---

### 768. reflect → evaluate

**Meaning:** to carefully assess something

**Examples:**
1. We evaluated the campaign's performance before renewing it.
2. The board will evaluate the CEO's proposal next week.

---

### 769. refuse → decline

**Meaning:** to politely reject an offer or request

**Examples:**
1. The client declined the revised proposal.
2. We declined the vendor's request for an early payment.

---

### 770. regard → consider

**Meaning:** to think about something carefully before deciding

**Examples:**
1. The committee will consider all proposals before voting.
2. We considered several vendors before making a decision.

---

### 771. regret → contrition

**Meaning:** a formal expression of sorrow or apology

**Examples:**
1. The company expressed contrition over the service outage.
2. Management offered contrition for the delayed response.

---

### 772. regular → routine

**Meaning:** happening on a consistent, scheduled basis

**Examples:**
1. We conduct routine audits every quarter.
2. Routine maintenance minimizes unexpected downtime.

---

### 773. reject → dismiss

**Meaning:** to refuse to accept or consider something

**Examples:**
1. The board dismissed the proposal due to budget concerns.
2. We dismissed two candidates after the first interview round.

---

### 774. relate → correlate

**Meaning:** to show a connection between two things

**Examples:**
1. Customer satisfaction correlates strongly with response time.
2. Sales figures correlate with our marketing spend.

---

### 775. relax → ease

**Meaning:** to reduce the strictness of a rule or requirement

**Examples:**
1. The company eased its return-to-office policy last month.
2. Regulators eased restrictions on cross-border trade.

---

### 776. release → unveil

**Meaning:** to make something publicly available for the first time

**Examples:**
1. We will unveil the new product line at the trade show.
2. The company unveiled its rebranding strategy this week.

---

### 777. reliable → consistent

**Meaning:** performing steadily and predictably over time

**Examples:**
1. We chose the supplier known for consistent delivery times.
2. The team delivered consistent results throughout the year.

---

### 778. relief → reprieve

**Meaning:** a temporary easing of a difficult situation

**Examples:**
1. The tax reprieve gave small businesses breathing room.
2. The extended deadline offered a welcome reprieve to the team.

---

### 779. rely → leverage

**Meaning:** to make use of something to gain an advantage

**Examples:**
1. We leveraged our supplier network to reduce lead times.
2. The startup leveraged social media to build brand awareness.

---

### 780. remain → endure

**Meaning:** to continue existing despite challenges

**Examples:**
1. Demand for our services has endured through the downturn.
2. The partnership has endured for over a decade.

---

## Section: S – Z

### 781. show → demonstrate

**Meaning:** to prove or make something clear through evidence or action

**Examples:**
1. The quarterly report demonstrates a steady increase in customer retention.
2. Can you demonstrate how the new dashboard will save the team time?

---

### 782. use → utilize

**Meaning:** to make practical use of something

**Examples:**
1. We need to utilize our existing resources before requesting a bigger budget.
2. The consultant recommended we utilize the CRM data to segment our outreach.

---

### 783. work → operate

**Meaning:** to function or run in a particular way

**Examples:**
1. The new supply chain will operate across three regional hubs.
2. Our finance team operates on a monthly reconciliation cycle.

---

### 784. want → desire

**Meaning:** to wish for or aim to achieve something

**Examples:**
1. Leadership has expressed a clear desire to expand into the APAC market.
2. The client's desire for faster turnaround shaped our new SLA.

---

### 785. start → initiate

**Meaning:** to begin a process or action

**Examples:**
1. We will initiate the onboarding process as soon as the contract is signed.
2. The board voted to initiate a full audit of vendor contracts.

---

### 786. stop → cease

**Meaning:** to bring something to an end

**Examples:**
1. The company will cease production of the legacy product line by Q3.
2. Please cease all outreach to this account until compliance completes its review.

---

### 787. see → observe

**Meaning:** to notice or take note of something

**Examples:**
1. We observed a sharp drop in engagement after the pricing change.
2. Analysts observed unusual trading volume ahead of the announcement.

---

### 788. say → state

**Meaning:** to express something clearly in words

**Examples:**
1. The CEO stated that layoffs are not currently under consideration.
2. Please state your objections in writing before the deadline.

---

### 789. send → transmit

**Meaning:** to pass something along to a recipient

**Examples:**
1. The invoice will be transmitted automatically once the order ships.
2. Please transmit the signed NDA to legal before the call.

---

### 790. talk → communicate

**Meaning:** to exchange information with others

**Examples:**
1. We need to communicate the policy change to all regional offices.
2. The manager communicated the new targets during Monday's stand-up.

---

### 791. tell → inform

**Meaning:** to give someone information

**Examples:**
1. Please inform the vendor of the revised delivery schedule.
2. HR will inform employees of the updated benefits plan next week.

---

### 792. take → acquire

**Meaning:** to obtain or gain possession of something

**Examples:**
1. The firm plans to acquire a smaller competitor before year end.
2. We acquired three new enterprise clients this quarter.

---

### 793. try → attempt

**Meaning:** to make an effort to do something

**Examples:**
1. The team will attempt to close the deal before the fiscal year ends.
2. We attempted a soft launch in one market before scaling nationally.

---

### 794. think → consider

**Meaning:** to think carefully about something before deciding

**Examples:**
1. Please consider the long-term costs before approving the vendor switch.
2. The committee is considering two proposals for the new office layout.

---

### 795. true → accurate

**Meaning:** correct and free from errors

**Examples:**
1. Make sure the figures in the forecast are accurate before submission.
2. The report provides an accurate picture of last quarter's spending.

---

### 796. top → premier

**Meaning:** the best or most important of its kind

**Examples:**
1. We remain the premier provider of logistics software in the region.
2. Our premier clients receive priority support and dedicated account managers.

---

### 797. team → workforce

**Meaning:** the group of people who work for an organization

**Examples:**
1. The workforce will need additional training before the system rollout.
2. Leadership is reviewing workforce capacity ahead of the new project.

---

### 798. test → evaluate

**Meaning:** to assess the quality or performance of something

**Examples:**
1. We will evaluate three vendors before selecting a long-term partner.
2. The product team is evaluating user feedback from the beta release.

---

### 799. total → aggregate

**Meaning:** the combined sum of all amounts or items

**Examples:**
1. The aggregate revenue across all business units exceeded projections.
2. Please share the aggregate cost of the marketing campaign by channel.

---

### 800. tool → instrument

**Meaning:** a means used to achieve a particular purpose

**Examples:**
1. The survey served as an instrument for measuring employee sentiment.
2. Pricing was used as an instrument to drive short-term demand.

---

### 801. wait → anticipate

**Meaning:** to expect something and prepare for it

**Examples:**
1. We anticipate a rise in demand ahead of the holiday season.
2. The team anticipated pushback from stakeholders and prepared a response.

---

### 802. watch → scrutinize

**Meaning:** to examine something closely and carefully

**Examples:**
1. Auditors will scrutinize every line item in the expense report.
2. The board plans to scrutinize the merger terms before approval.

---

### 803. win → clinch

**Meaning:** to succeed in securing something after effort

**Examples:**
1. The sales team clinched the contract after months of negotiation.
2. We clinched a partnership with the region's largest distributor.

---

### 804. write → draft

**Meaning:** to prepare a written version of something

**Examples:**
1. Legal will draft the updated terms of service by Friday.
2. Please draft a brief summary for the executive committee.

---

### 805. wrong → erroneous

**Meaning:** incorrect or based on a mistake

**Examples:**
1. The erroneous figures in the invoice caused a billing dispute.
2. We issued a correction after identifying erroneous data in the report.

---

### 806. worry → concern

**Meaning:** a feeling of unease about a possible problem

**Examples:**
1. Investors raised concern over the company's rising debt levels.
2. Employee concern about job security grew after the announcement.

---

### 807. understand → comprehend

**Meaning:** to fully grasp the meaning or implications of something

**Examples:**
1. New hires must comprehend the compliance policy before onboarding.
2. The training helped staff comprehend the updated workflow.

---

### 808. small → minor

**Meaning:** of little importance or size

**Examples:**
1. The update introduces only minor changes to the checkout process.
2. We noted a minor discrepancy in the shipping records.

---

### 809. sure → confident

**Meaning:** certain and free of doubt

**Examples:**
1. The board is confident the acquisition will close by December.
2. We are confident in the accuracy of the revised projections.

---

### 810. set → establish

**Meaning:** to put something in place formally

**Examples:**
1. We need to establish clear KPIs before the campaign launches.
2. The company established a new compliance department this year.

---

### 811. value → prioritize

**Meaning:** to treat something as more important than other things

**Examples:**
1. Our clients prioritize transparency over speed of delivery.
2. The team is prioritizing retention initiatives this quarter.

---

### 812. view → perspective

**Meaning:** a particular way of considering a situation

**Examples:**
1. The consultant offered a fresh perspective on our pricing strategy.
2. We gathered customer perspective through a series of interviews.

---

### 813. support → facilitate

**Meaning:** to make an action or process easier

**Examples:**
1. The new platform will facilitate faster cross-team collaboration.
2. HR facilitated the transition for employees moving to hybrid work.

---

### 814. share → disseminate

**Meaning:** to spread information widely among a group

**Examples:**
1. Please disseminate the updated policy to all department heads.
2. The research findings were disseminated at the annual conference.

---

### 815. spend → allocate

**Meaning:** to assign resources for a specific purpose

**Examples:**
1. We will allocate additional budget to the marketing team next quarter.
2. The project manager allocated resources based on task priority.

---

### 816. stay → remain

**Meaning:** to continue to exist in the same state

**Examples:**
1. Pricing will remain unchanged through the end of the fiscal year.
2. The company remains committed to its sustainability targets.

---

### 817. sell → market

**Meaning:** to promote and offer a product for sale

**Examples:**
1. We plan to market the new service to small business owners first.
2. The agency was hired to market the product across social channels.

---

### 818. suggest → recommend

**Meaning:** to put forward an idea as a course of action

**Examples:**
1. The consultant recommended restructuring the sales territories.
2. We recommend upgrading the servers before the next release.

---

### 819. strong → robust

**Meaning:** sturdy and effective under pressure

**Examples:**
1. The company reported robust growth despite market headwinds.
2. We need a robust backup plan in case the vendor falls through.

---

### 820. simple → straightforward

**Meaning:** easy to understand or do

**Examples:**
1. The onboarding process is straightforward for new employees.
2. We proposed a straightforward pricing model with no hidden fees.

---

### 821. same → identical

**Meaning:** exactly alike in every detail

**Examples:**
1. The two proposals contain nearly identical terms.
2. Ensure all branches use identical reporting templates.

---

### 822. safe → secure

**Meaning:** protected from risk or danger

**Examples:**
1. Customer data is stored on a secure, encrypted server.
2. We want to keep the company's financial position secure amid uncertainty.

---

### 823. save → conserve

**Meaning:** to use resources carefully to avoid waste

**Examples:**
1. The new process helps conserve both time and operating costs.
2. Management asked all departments to conserve travel spending this quarter.

---

### 824. study → analyze

**Meaning:** to examine something in detail to understand it

**Examples:**
1. The team will analyze customer churn data before the renewal cycle.
2. Analysts analyzed the market before recommending the acquisition.

---

### 825. speed → accelerate

**Meaning:** to make something happen faster

**Examples:**
1. We aim to accelerate delivery times with the new logistics partner.
2. The funding round will accelerate product development.

---

### 826. staff → personnel

**Meaning:** the people employed by an organization

**Examples:**
1. Additional personnel will be hired to support the new region.
2. The personnel department is updating the leave policy.

---

### 827. wish → aspire

**Meaning:** to hope to achieve something in the future

**Examples:**
1. The startup aspires to become the market leader within five years.
2. Our team aspires to deliver best-in-class customer service.

---

### 828. weak → deficient

**Meaning:** lacking in strength or quality needed

**Examples:**
1. The audit found the internal controls to be deficient.
2. The proposal was rejected due to deficient risk analysis.

---

### 829. warn → alert

**Meaning:** to inform someone of a possible danger

**Examples:**
1. The compliance team will alert managers to any policy violations.
2. We alerted customers about the upcoming service outage.

---

### 830. worth → merit

**Meaning:** the value or significance something deserves

**Examples:**
1. The proposal has real merit and should go before the board.
2. The committee debated the merit of each candidate's plan.

---

### 831. wide → expansive

**Meaning:** covering a large range or area

**Examples:**
1. The company offers an expansive portfolio of financial products.
2. We conducted an expansive review of vendor contracts this year.

---

### 832. teach → instruct

**Meaning:** to give someone knowledge or training

**Examples:**
1. The onboarding program instructs new hires on company systems.
2. Managers were instructed to complete the compliance course by Friday.

---

### 833. tough → challenging

**Meaning:** difficult and demanding effort to overcome

**Examples:**
1. This has been a challenging quarter due to supply shortages.
2. The negotiation proved challenging given the vendor's fixed terms.

---

### 834. tiny → negligible

**Meaning:** so small as to be not worth considering

**Examples:**
1. The price adjustment had a negligible impact on total sales.
2. The margin of error in the forecast is negligible.

---

### 835. tired → fatigued

**Meaning:** worn out from excessive effort or strain

**Examples:**
1. The team reported feeling fatigued after the intense product launch.
2. Burnout surveys showed employees were fatigued by constant overtime.

---

### 836. together → collectively

**Meaning:** as a group acting jointly

**Examples:**
1. The departments collectively exceeded their annual targets.
2. We collectively agreed to postpone the launch by two weeks.

---

---

### 973. subtract → deduct

**Meaning:** to take away an amount from a total

**Examples:**
1. Taxes will be deducted automatically from the final invoice.
2. We deducted the discount before calculating the total due.

---

### 974. succeed → prevail

**Meaning:** to achieve success despite difficulty

**Examples:**
1. Our proposal prevailed over three competing bids.
2. The company prevailed in a competitive market thanks to strong branding.

---

### 975. tally → reconcile

**Meaning:** to make sure two sets of figures match

**Examples:**
1. Finance reconciles the monthly accounts before closing the books.
2. We reconciled the invoice with the purchase order before payment.

---

### 976. tender → submission

**Meaning:** a formal offer submitted for a contract or project

**Examples:**
1. Our submission was selected among twelve competing bids.
2. The deadline for the tender submission is next Friday.

---

### 977. thick → dense

**Meaning:** closely packed or concentrated

**Examples:**
1. The report contains dense technical detail meant for specialists.
2. The dense schedule left little room for unexpected delays.

---

### 978. thoughtful → considerate

**Meaning:** showing care and attention to others' needs

**Examples:**
1. The manager took a considerate approach when announcing the changes.
2. Clients appreciated the considerate handling of their complaint.

---

### 979. thrust → impetus

**Meaning:** a driving force behind an action or change

**Examples:**
1. Customer complaints provided the impetus for the redesign.
2. The new CEO gave fresh impetus to the digital transformation.

---

### 980. tighten → constrict

**Meaning:** to make something more restrictive or limited

**Examples:**
1. The new policy constricts spending across all departments.
2. Regulatory changes have constricted the lending market.

---

### 981. timid → hesitant

**Meaning:** showing a lack of confidence in taking action

**Examples:**
1. Investors remained hesitant despite the improved earnings.
2. The team was hesitant to adopt the untested technology.

---

### 982. topple → overturn

**Meaning:** to cause something to fail or be reversed

**Examples:**
1. The board voted to overturn the previous pricing decision.
2. A single bad quarter can overturn months of investor confidence.

---

### 983. tumble → plummet

**Meaning:** to fall suddenly and sharply

**Examples:**
1. Shares plummeted after the disappointing earnings call.
2. Customer satisfaction plummeted following the service outage.

---

### 984. tricky → complex

**Meaning:** involving many interconnected parts and difficult to handle

**Examples:**
1. The tax implications of the merger are unusually complex.
2. We hired outside counsel to navigate the complex regulations.

---

### 985. typical → conventional

**Meaning:** following usual or standard practice

**Examples:**
1. We moved away from the conventional hiring process for this role.
2. The proposal challenges conventional thinking about remote work.

---

### 986. topical → relevant

**Meaning:** closely connected to the matter at hand

**Examples:**
1. The panel discussion focused on the most relevant industry trends.
2. Keep the presentation relevant to the client's core concerns.

---

### 987. stage → juncture

**Meaning:** a particular point in a process or period of time

**Examples:**
1. At this juncture, we recommend pausing the expansion plan.
2. The project reached a critical juncture after the funding delay.

---

### 988. steer → direct

**Meaning:** to guide the course of something

**Examples:**
1. The new director will direct the company's overseas strategy.
2. Leadership directed the team toward a more customer-first approach.

---

### 989. stir → provoke

**Meaning:** to cause a strong reaction or response

**Examples:**
1. The pricing announcement provoked immediate backlash from customers.
2. The proposal provoked healthy debate among the board members.

---

### 990. strip → eliminate

**Meaning:** to remove something completely

**Examples:**
1. The restructuring eliminated several layers of middle management.
2. We eliminated redundant steps from the approval workflow.

---

### 991. stumble → falter

**Meaning:** to lose momentum or make a mistake

**Examples:**
1. The rollout faltered due to unresolved technical issues.
2. Sales faltered in the second half of the quarter.

---

### 992. timely → punctual

**Meaning:** happening at the right or expected time

**Examples:**
1. Punctual delivery is a key part of our service guarantee.
2. We rely on punctual reporting to keep the board informed.

---

### 993. truthful → forthright

**Meaning:** direct and honest in speech

**Examples:**
1. The CFO was forthright about the challenges facing the division.
2. We value forthright feedback from our customers, even when critical.

---

### 994. trigger → precipitate

**Meaning:** to cause something to happen suddenly

**Examples:**
1. The supply shortage precipitated a sharp rise in prices.
2. The scandal precipitated a leadership shake-up at the firm.

---

### 995. twist → variation

**Meaning:** a change or deviation from the usual approach

**Examples:**
1. We introduced a variation on the standard pricing model for enterprise clients.
2. The campaign offered a fresh variation on last year's theme.

---

### 996. tune → calibrate

**Meaning:** to adjust something precisely for accuracy or performance

**Examples:**
1. We calibrated the forecasting model using last year's actual sales.
2. The team calibrated its targets after reviewing market conditions.

---

### 997. thankful → appreciative

**Meaning:** feeling or showing gratitude

**Examples:**
1. The team was appreciative of leadership's support during the transition.
2. Customers were appreciative of the proactive communication during the delay.

---

### 998. talkative → loquacious

**Meaning:** tending to talk a great deal

**Examples:**
1. The keynote speaker was famously loquacious, running well over time.
2. A loquacious client can make it hard to keep meetings on schedule.

---

### 999. spirit → ethos

**Meaning:** the characteristic values and attitudes of a group

**Examples:**
1. The company's ethos centers on transparency and accountability.
2. Leadership worked hard to preserve the startup's collaborative ethos after the acquisition.

---

### 1000. swell → expand

**Meaning:** to grow larger in size or number

**Examples:**
1. The customer base expanded rapidly after the product launch.
2. We expect the team to expand significantly next fiscal year.

---

## Section: A – C (More)

### 1001. able → proficient

**Meaning:** having the skill and knowledge to do something well

**Examples:**
1. She is proficient in financial modeling and can build a forecast in under an hour.
2. New hires must be proficient in the CRM system before handling client accounts.

---

### 1002. absent → unavailable

**Meaning:** not present or able to attend

**Examples:**
1. The finance director was unavailable for the budget review, so the meeting was rescheduled.
2. Please flag your calendar as unavailable if you cannot join the client call.

---

### 1003. abuse → exploit

**Meaning:** to use something unfairly for personal gain

**Examples:**
1. The audit found that one manager had exploited the expense policy for personal purchases.
2. Competitors may exploit any delay in our product launch to gain market share.

---

### 1004. accompany → escort

**Meaning:** to go with someone, especially to provide guidance or support

**Examples:**
1. A senior consultant will escort the new client through the onboarding process.
2. HR will escort the visiting auditors around the facility this afternoon.

---

### 1005. accomplish → fulfill

**Meaning:** to complete or achieve something, especially a goal or duty

**Examples:**
1. The team fulfilled every milestone in the project charter ahead of schedule.
2. We need this hire to fulfill the requirements of the compliance role by Q1.

---

### 1006. account → substantiate

**Meaning:** to give reasons or evidence that support a claim

**Examples:**
1. The vendor was asked to substantiate the price increase with supporting cost data.
2. Please substantiate this variance before we submit the report to the board.

---

### 1007. achieve → realize

**Meaning:** to make a goal or plan actually happen

**Examples:**
1. The merger allowed both companies to realize significant cost synergies.
2. Our sales team realized its annual target two months early.

---

### 1008. acquire → procure

**Meaning:** to obtain something, often through effort or a formal process

**Examples:**
1. The procurement team was tasked to procure new servers before the fiscal year ends.
2. We procured the licensing rights after months of negotiation.

---

### 1009. across → throughout

**Meaning:** in every part of something

**Examples:**
1. The new policy will be rolled out throughout the organization by March.
2. Employee engagement has improved throughout the company since the survey.

---

### 1010. active → proactive

**Meaning:** taking action in advance rather than reacting to events

**Examples:**
1. Our support team is proactive about flagging issues before customers notice them.
2. Leadership wants a more proactive approach to managing supply chain risk.

---

### 1011. activity → initiative

**Meaning:** a planned action or project, especially in business

**Examples:**
1. The sustainability initiative reduced packaging waste by twenty percent.
2. HR launched a new wellness initiative for remote employees.

---

### 1012. adapt → recalibrate

**Meaning:** to adjust or fine-tune something to new conditions

**Examples:**
1. We had to recalibrate our sales targets after the market downturn.
2. The team recalibrated its strategy following the competitor's product launch.

---

### 1013. advance → expedite

**Meaning:** to make something happen faster

**Examples:**
1. Can you expedite the shipment so it arrives before the trade show?
2. Legal agreed to expedite the contract review given the tight deadline.

---

### 1014. advantage → leverage

**Meaning:** an added strength or edge used to achieve a goal

**Examples:**
1. Our early adoption of AI gives us a real leverage over slower competitors.
2. Use the partnership as leverage when negotiating better terms with suppliers.

---

### 1015. advertise → promote

**Meaning:** to publicize something in order to increase interest or sales

**Examples:**
1. The agency was hired to promote the new product line across social media.
2. We plan to promote the webinar through our partner newsletters.

---

### 1016. advise → counsel

**Meaning:** to give professional guidance or advice

**Examples:**
1. The consultants counseled us against expanding into that region this year.
2. Legal counseled the board on the risks of the proposed acquisition.

---

### 1017. affect → impact

**Meaning:** to have a noticeable effect on something

**Examples:**
1. Rising fuel costs will impact our logistics margins next quarter.
2. The new regulation could impact how we price our services.

---

### 1018. against → counter to

**Meaning:** in opposition to; contrary to

**Examples:**
1. Delaying the launch runs counter to our stated growth strategy.
2. The proposal is counter to the recommendations in last month's audit.

---

### 1019. age → vintage

**Meaning:** the year or period something was produced, used to describe how old something is

**Examples:**
1. The vintage of this dataset limits its usefulness for current forecasting.
2. We should retire equipment past a certain vintage to reduce maintenance costs.

---

### 1020. agenda → roadmap

**Meaning:** a plan outlining the steps toward a goal over time

**Examples:**
1. The product roadmap outlines every release planned through next year.
2. Leadership shared the digital transformation roadmap at the all-hands meeting.

---

### 1021. aggressive → assertive

**Meaning:** confident, direct, and forceful in pursuing a goal

**Examples:**
1. We need an assertive pricing strategy to compete with the new entrant.
2. The sales rep took an assertive approach to close the deal before quarter-end.

---

### 1022. alarm → apprehension

**Meaning:** a feeling of worry about something that might happen

**Examples:**
1. Investors expressed apprehension about the company's rising debt levels.
2. There was some apprehension among staff about the upcoming restructuring.

---

### 1023. allocate → earmark

**Meaning:** to set aside a resource for a specific purpose

**Examples:**
1. The board earmarked two million dollars for the new R&D lab.
2. We have earmarked additional headcount for the customer success team.

---

### 1024. ally → advocate

**Meaning:** a person who actively supports a cause or another person's interests

**Examples:**
1. We need an internal advocate to champion this project with senior leadership.
2. The vendor became a valuable advocate for our product during the trade show.

---

### 1025. almost → virtually

**Meaning:** very nearly; almost completely

**Examples:**
1. The project is virtually complete, with only final testing remaining.
2. Customer complaints have virtually disappeared since the update shipped.

---

### 1026. ambitious → aspirational

**Meaning:** reflecting high hopes or ambitious goals

**Examples:**
1. The five-year plan sets aspirational revenue targets for every division.
2. Our aspirational goal is to become carbon neutral by 2030.

---

### 1027. angle → vantage point

**Meaning:** a particular way of viewing or approaching a situation

**Examples:**
1. From an investor's vantage point, the acquisition looks undervalued.
2. Consider the customer's vantage point before finalizing the messaging.

---

### 1028. angry → irate

**Meaning:** extremely angry

**Examples:**
1. An irate customer called demanding a full refund for the defective order.
2. The irate shareholder questioned the board's decision during the AGM.

---

### 1029. annual → recurring

**Meaning:** happening repeatedly on a regular cycle

**Examples:**
1. The recurring performance review helps track progress against yearly goals.
2. We budgeted for the recurring licensing fee in this year's forecast.

---

### 1030. appeal → allure

**Meaning:** the quality of being attractive or tempting

**Examples:**
1. The allure of a flexible schedule helped us win top candidates over competitors.
2. Marketing highlighted the brand's allure in the new campaign.

---

### 1031. appetite → propensity

**Meaning:** a natural tendency or inclination toward something

**Examples:**
1. The fund has a low propensity for risk, favoring stable, long-term returns.
2. Leadership's propensity for caution slowed the product's approval process.

---

### 1032. applaud → commend

**Meaning:** to praise someone formally for their work

**Examples:**
1. The CEO commended the operations team for their handling of the crisis.
2. We commend the vendor for delivering ahead of the agreed timeline.

---

### 1033. appliance → apparatus

**Meaning:** equipment or machinery used for a particular purpose, often technical

**Examples:**
1. The factory upgraded its manufacturing apparatus to increase output.
2. New testing apparatus was installed in the quality control lab.

---

### 1034. approachable → amiable

**Meaning:** friendly and easy to talk to

**Examples:**
1. Clients appreciated how amiable the new account manager was during onboarding.
2. An amiable leadership style helped rebuild trust after the reorganization.

---

### 1035. aptitude → acumen

**Meaning:** sharp, practical ability to make good judgments, especially in a field

**Examples:**
1. Her financial acumen made her the obvious choice to lead the budget review.
2. The candidate demonstrated strong commercial acumen during the interview.

---

### 1036. archive → repository

**Meaning:** a place where information or items are stored

**Examples:**
1. All contracts are stored in a central repository for easy retrieval.
2. The IT team migrated old files to a secure cloud repository.

---

### 1037. arena → domain

**Meaning:** a particular field of activity or knowledge

**Examples:**
1. The company is expanding into the healthcare domain this year.
2. Cybersecurity remains a critical domain for every enterprise today.

---

### 1038. arise → emerge

**Meaning:** to come into existence or become apparent

**Examples:**
1. New risks may emerge as the project scope expands.
2. A clear market leader emerged after the third round of funding.

---

### 1039. arrogant → condescending

**Meaning:** acting as if one is superior to others

**Examples:**
1. The client felt the sales pitch came across as condescending rather than confident.
2. A condescending tone in the feedback session damaged team morale.

---

### 1040. art → craftsmanship

**Meaning:** skill and artistry in producing something, especially in design work

**Examples:**
1. The craftsmanship behind the packaging design set the brand apart.
2. Clients pay a premium for the craftsmanship of our bespoke reports.

---

### 1041. artificial → synthetic

**Meaning:** made through combined processes rather than occurring naturally

**Examples:**
1. The demo relied on synthetic data rather than real customer records.
2. Analysts questioned whether the earnings growth was synthetic or sustainable.

---

### 1042. aside → tangential

**Meaning:** only slightly related to the main topic

**Examples:**
1. That's a tangential point, let's return to the main agenda item.
2. The report included several tangential observations that diluted its focus.

---

### 1043. aspect → facet

**Meaning:** one particular part or feature of something

**Examples:**
1. Pricing is just one facet of the overall customer experience.
2. The consultant examined every facet of the supply chain during the review.

---

### 1044. assemble → convene

**Meaning:** to come together, especially for a meeting

**Examples:**
1. The steering committee will convene next Tuesday to finalize the budget.
2. Department heads convened to discuss the reorganization plan.

---

### 1045. assert → posit

**Meaning:** to put forward an idea or claim as true

**Examples:**
1. The report posits that remote work has improved overall productivity.
2. Analysts posited that the slowdown was temporary and sector-specific.

---

### 1046. asset → holding

**Meaning:** something owned that has value, such as property or investments

**Examples:**
1. The company's largest holding is its downtown headquarters building.
2. The investment fund diversified its holdings across several industries.

---

### 1047. assortment → portfolio

**Meaning:** a range or collection of items, such as products or investments

**Examples:**
1. We expanded our product portfolio to include three new price tiers.
2. The firm manages a diverse portfolio of client accounts across sectors.

---

### 1048. assure → affirm

**Meaning:** to state something confidently as true

**Examples:**
1. The CFO affirmed that the company remains on track to meet its targets.
2. Management affirmed its commitment to the sustainability roadmap.

---

### 1049. astonish → astound

**Meaning:** to surprise someone greatly

**Examples:**
1. The quarterly growth numbers astounded even our most optimistic analysts.
2. The speed of the product launch astounded our competitors.

---

### 1050. atmosphere → ambiance

**Meaning:** the overall feeling or mood of a place

**Examples:**
1. The office redesign created a more collaborative ambiance for the team.
2. The retail store's ambiance is central to the brand's premium positioning.

---

### 1051. attitude → disposition

**Meaning:** a person's usual way of thinking or feeling about things

**Examples:**
1. Her positive disposition helped the team stay motivated through the deadline crunch.
2. The hiring panel looks for a disposition suited to fast-paced environments.

---

### 1052. attractive → compelling

**Meaning:** strongly capturing attention or interest

**Examples:**
1. The proposal presented a compelling case for entering the Asian market.
2. Investors found the startup's growth story compelling enough to lead the round.

---

### 1053. audience → constituency

**Meaning:** a group of people who are targeted or represented, such as customers

**Examples:**
1. This campaign is designed to reach a younger constituency of buyers.
2. The policy change affects a broad constituency of small business owners.

---

### 1054. audit → scrutinize

**Meaning:** to examine something closely and critically

**Examples:**
1. The compliance team will scrutinize every expense report filed this quarter.
2. Regulators scrutinized the bank's lending practices after the complaint.

---

### 1055. authentic → bona fide

**Meaning:** real and not fake; genuine

**Examples:**
1. Customers respond best to bona fide stories about how the product is made.
2. The certificate confirms this is a bona fide partnership agreement.

---

### 1056. author → originator

**Meaning:** the person who creates or initiates something

**Examples:**
1. She was the originator of the loyalty program that boosted retention by 15 percent.
2. The consulting firm credited its founder as the originator of the framework.

---

### 1057. authority → jurisdiction

**Meaning:** the official power or right to make decisions in a certain area

**Examples:**
1. Approving vendor contracts falls under the procurement director's jurisdiction.
2. The regional office has jurisdiction over hiring decisions below the director level.

---

### 1058. automatic → autonomous

**Meaning:** operating independently without human control

**Examples:**
1. The warehouse now uses autonomous robots to manage inventory picking.
2. We are piloting autonomous approval workflows for low-risk purchase orders.

---

### 1059. await → anticipate

**Meaning:** to expect something and prepare for it

**Examples:**
1. We anticipate a spike in demand once the holiday campaign launches.
2. Analysts anticipate further rate cuts before the end of the year.

---

### 1060. award → accolade

**Meaning:** an award or recognition given for an achievement

**Examples:**
1. The firm received an industry accolade for its customer service standards.
2. Winning the accolade boosted employee morale across the department.

---

### 1061. badge → credential

**Meaning:** a qualification or proof of authority, identity, or achievement

**Examples:**
1. Employees must scan a valid credential to access the server room.
2. The certification credential is now a requirement for senior analyst roles.

---

### 1062. baggage → encumbrance

**Meaning:** something that burdens or hinders progress

**Examples:**
1. Legacy systems remain a significant encumbrance to our digital transformation.
2. The merger was slowed by the encumbrance of unresolved legal disputes.

---

### 1063. bait → enticement

**Meaning:** something used to attract or tempt someone

**Examples:**
1. The free trial acts as an enticement to convert visitors into paying customers.
2. A generous signing bonus was the enticement that closed the deal with the candidate.

---

### 1064. bench → standby

**Meaning:** available and ready to act if needed

**Examples:**
1. We keep two engineers on standby during every product launch.
2. The agency assigned a standby team in case the campaign needed rapid changes.

---

### 1065. beware → vigilant

**Meaning:** keeping careful watch for possible danger or problems

**Examples:**
1. Finance teams must remain vigilant for signs of invoice fraud.
2. Stay vigilant about phishing emails targeting the finance department.

---

### 1066. beyond → transcend

**Meaning:** to go beyond the usual limits of something

**Examples:**
1. The brand's appeal transcends age groups and geographic markets.
2. Our partnership needs to transcend a simple vendor relationship.

---

### 1067. bias → predisposition

**Meaning:** a tendency to favor one thing over another, often unconsciously

**Examples:**
1. The hiring panel was trained to recognize its own predisposition toward certain schools.
2. Investors showed a predisposition toward companies with strong ESG scores.

---

### 1068. bid → tender

**Meaning:** a formal offer to carry out work or supply goods at a stated price

**Examples:**
1. Three contractors submitted a tender for the office renovation project.
2. We plan to submit a competitive tender for the government contract.

---

### 1069. bind → obligate

**Meaning:** to legally or morally require someone to do something

**Examples:**
1. The contract obligates both parties to a twelve-month notice period.
2. Signing the agreement obligates the supplier to meet strict delivery deadlines.

---

### 1070. blank → vacant

**Meaning:** empty or not filled, such as an unassigned role

**Examples:**
1. The regional director position remains vacant after the recent departure.
2. Please do not leave any vacant fields on the compliance form.

---

### 1071. blast → surge

**Meaning:** a sudden, strong increase in activity or intensity

**Examples:**
1. The launch generated a surge in website traffic within the first hour.
2. We saw a surge of orders after the influencer campaign went live.

---

### 1072. blind → oblivious

**Meaning:** not aware of what is happening around

**Examples:**
1. The team was oblivious to the competitor's pricing change until customers mentioned it.
2. Management remained oblivious to the growing dissatisfaction among frontline staff.

---

### 1073. block → impede

**Meaning:** to slow down or stop progress

**Examples:**
1. Outdated approval processes continue to impede project delivery.
2. A shortage of raw materials could impede our production schedule.

---

### 1074. blow → setback

**Meaning:** a difficulty or problem that hinders progress

**Examples:**
1. Losing the key supplier was a major setback for the launch timeline.
2. The failed pilot was a setback, but the team quickly regrouped.

---

### 1075. blunder → misstep

**Meaning:** a mistake, especially one that causes embarrassment

**Examples:**
1. The pricing misstep cost the company several key accounts.
2. Leadership acknowledged the communication misstep during the layoffs.

---

### 1076. blurry → ambiguous

**Meaning:** unclear or open to more than one interpretation

**Examples:**
1. The contract language around termination fees was ambiguous.
2. Please clarify the ambiguous wording before we send this to legal.

---

### 1077. boast → tout

**Meaning:** to promote or praise something enthusiastically

**Examples:**
1. The press release touted the product's industry-leading battery life.
2. Competitors are touting similar features, so we need a stronger differentiator.

---

### 1078. body → corpus

**Meaning:** a large collected body of work or information

**Examples:**
1. We reviewed the entire corpus of customer feedback before redesigning the app.
2. The research team built a corpus of case studies to support the proposal.

---

### 1079. bolster → fortify

**Meaning:** to make something stronger

**Examples:**
1. The new hires will fortify our engineering capacity ahead of the launch.
2. Additional funding will fortify the company's cash reserves.

---

### 1080. bombard → inundate

**Meaning:** to overwhelm someone with a large amount of something

**Examples:**
1. Customer support was inundated with calls after the outage.
2. Please don't inundate the client with too many follow-up emails.

---

### 1081. bonus → premium

**Meaning:** an extra amount paid or added beyond the standard amount

**Examples:**
1. Employees receive a premium for completing certifications ahead of schedule.
2. Customers pay a premium for expedited shipping options.

---

### 1082. book → reserve

**Meaning:** to arrange to have something set aside for use at a future time

**Examples:**
1. Please reserve the conference room for the board meeting next week.
2. We reserved additional server capacity ahead of the product launch.

---

### 1083. boom → upswing

**Meaning:** a period of growth or increase, especially economic

**Examples:**
1. The housing market saw a strong upswing following the rate cuts.
2. An upswing in demand pushed the factory to add a second shift.

---

### 1084. border → periphery

**Meaning:** the outer edge or limit of an area

**Examples:**
1. These smaller markets remain on the periphery of our expansion plans.
2. The new regulation affects businesses even on the periphery of the financial sector.

---

### 1085. borrow → draw upon

**Meaning:** to make use of a resource, often temporarily

**Examples:**
1. We drew upon the marketing team's expertise to launch the campaign quickly.
2. The startup drew upon its founders' savings to cover early operating costs.

---

### 1086. bottleneck → constraint

**Meaning:** a limitation that restricts capacity or progress

**Examples:**
1. Approval delays remain the biggest constraint on our delivery timeline.
2. Warehouse space is the main constraint limiting further growth.

---

### 1087. bottom → nadir

**Meaning:** the lowest point of something

**Examples:**
1. Sales hit their nadir in the second quarter before rebounding strongly.
2. Employee morale reached its nadir during the restructuring announcement.

---

### 1088. bounce → rebound

**Meaning:** to recover quickly after a setback

**Examples:**
1. The stock rebounded within days of the earnings miss.
2. Customer confidence rebounded once the service outage was resolved.

---

### 1089. brace → gird

**Meaning:** to prepare mentally for something difficult

**Examples:**
1. The team is girding for a challenging renewal season with tighter budgets.
2. Leadership asked employees to gird for a period of significant change.

---

### 1090. brake → curb

**Meaning:** to restrain or reduce something

**Examples:**
1. The board decided to curb discretionary spending until revenue stabilizes.
2. New policies aim to curb excessive overtime across departments.

---

### 1091. branch → subsidiary

**Meaning:** a business unit owned or controlled by a parent company

**Examples:**
1. The European subsidiary reported stronger growth than the parent company.
2. We are setting up a new subsidiary to handle regional distribution.

---

### 1092. brash → audacious

**Meaning:** bold and confident, sometimes recklessly so

**Examples:**
1. The startup's audacious pricing strategy rattled established competitors.
2. Analysts called the acquisition an audacious bet on future growth.

---

### 1093. brave → intrepid

**Meaning:** fearless and adventurous

**Examples:**
1. The intrepid founder relocated the entire team to enter a new market.
2. Our intrepid sales team pursued the account despite three prior rejections.

---

### 1094. breach → violation

**Meaning:** an act of breaking a rule, law, or agreement

**Examples:**
1. The audit uncovered a violation of the data retention policy.
2. Any violation of the confidentiality clause could result in legal action.

---

### 1095. breadth → scope

**Meaning:** the range or extent of something covered

**Examples:**
1. The consultant's report covers the full scope of our operational risks.
2. We underestimated the scope of the integration when planning the timeline.

---

### 1096. breath → respite

**Meaning:** a short period of rest or relief

**Examples:**
1. The delayed launch gave the engineering team a much-needed respite.
2. A brief respite between projects helped reduce burnout across the team.

---

### 1097. breed → cultivate

**Meaning:** to develop or encourage something over time

**Examples:**
1. Regular recognition helps cultivate loyalty among top performers.
2. The mentorship program was designed to cultivate future leaders.

---

### 1098. breeze → seamless

**Meaning:** smooth and without difficulty or interruption

**Examples:**
1. The migration to the new platform was seamless for most customers.
2. We aim for a seamless handover between the sales and onboarding teams.

---

### 1099. bribe → kickback

**Meaning:** an illicit payment made in exchange for favorable treatment

**Examples:**
1. The investigation revealed a kickback scheme involving two regional managers.
2. Our compliance policy strictly prohibits accepting any kickback from vendors.

---

### 1100. bridge → conduit

**Meaning:** a channel or means by which something is transmitted between parties

**Examples:**
1. The account manager serves as the conduit between the client and engineering.
2. This partnership acts as a conduit for entering the Latin American market.

---

### 1101. brilliant → exceptional

**Meaning:** unusually good; outstanding

**Examples:**
1. The team delivered exceptional results despite the shortened timeline.
2. Her exceptional presentation secured buy-in from the entire board.

---

### 1102. brisk → vigorous

**Meaning:** energetic and active

**Examples:**
1. The holiday season brought vigorous demand for the new product line.
2. Trading was vigorous following the surprise earnings announcement.

---

### 1103. brittle → precarious

**Meaning:** not securely stable; likely to fail

**Examples:**
1. The company's precarious cash position raised concerns among investors.
2. Relying on a single supplier left the supply chain in a precarious position.

---

### 1104. broadcast → disseminate

**Meaning:** to spread information widely

**Examples:**
1. HR will disseminate the new policy to all employees by email this week.
2. The findings were disseminated across every regional office.

---

### 1105. brochure → collateral

**Meaning:** printed or digital promotional materials used in marketing

**Examples:**
1. The design team updated all sales collateral ahead of the conference.
2. Please review the collateral before we send it to the client.

---

### 1106. browse → peruse

**Meaning:** to read or examine something carefully or casually

**Examples:**
1. Take a moment to peruse the attached proposal before our call.
2. Investors perused the annual report for signs of margin improvement.

---

### 1107. brush → refine

**Meaning:** to improve something through practice or minor changes

**Examples:**
1. The team refined its pitch after feedback from the first round of investors.
2. We plan to refine the onboarding process based on the survey results.

---

### 1108. brutal → unforgiving

**Meaning:** extremely harsh or difficult

**Examples:**
1. The competitive landscape in this sector remains unforgiving for new entrants.
2. An unforgiving deadline forced the team to work through the weekend.

---

### 1109. bubble → overvaluation

**Meaning:** a situation where prices rise far beyond real value

**Examples:**
1. Analysts warned of an overvaluation in the tech sector before the correction.
2. The report cautioned against the overvaluation of speculative assets.

---

### 1110. buddy → counterpart

**Meaning:** a person holding a similar position in another team or organization

**Examples:**
1. I connected with my counterpart at the partner firm to align on the timeline.
2. Each new hire is paired with a counterpart from a related department.

---

### 1111. budge → concede

**Meaning:** to admit or give way on a point, especially in negotiation

**Examples:**
1. The supplier refused to concede on the minimum order quantity.
2. After a long discussion, the vendor conceded on the payment terms.

---

### 1112. buffer → margin

**Meaning:** extra space or amount allowed for safety

**Examples:**
1. We built in a two-week margin to absorb any shipping delays.
2. Keep a cash margin in case the client payment is delayed.

---

### 1113. building → infrastructure

**Meaning:** the underlying structures and facilities needed for operation

**Examples:**
1. The company invested heavily in cloud infrastructure last year.
2. Aging infrastructure was cited as a risk to the plant's productivity.

---

### 1114. bulletin → communique

**Meaning:** an official announcement or report

**Examples:**
1. The executive team issued a communique explaining the restructuring plan.
2. A brief communique was sent to shareholders following the board meeting.

---

### 1115. bully → intimidate

**Meaning:** to frighten someone into doing something

**Examples:**
1. Employees felt intimidated into working unpaid overtime during the crunch.
2. The company was accused of trying to intimidate the whistleblower into silence.

---

### 1116. bundle → consolidate

**Meaning:** to combine multiple things into one

**Examples:**
1. We consolidated three separate tools into a single software platform.
2. The proposal consolidates all regional budgets into one master plan.

---

### 1117. burn → deplete

**Meaning:** to use up a resource over time

**Examples:**
1. The startup is depleting its cash reserves faster than projected.
2. Overtime hours are depleting the team's energy heading into quarter close.

---

### 1118. burnout → exhaustion

**Meaning:** extreme tiredness, often from prolonged stress

**Examples:**
1. Management introduced flexible hours to address rising employee exhaustion.
2. The survey flagged exhaustion as the top concern among support staff.

---

### 1119. burst → spike

**Meaning:** a sudden, sharp increase

**Examples:**
1. We saw a spike in returns after the packaging change.
2. The campaign caused a temporary spike in website traffic.

---

### 1120. bury → obscure

**Meaning:** to hide or make something less noticeable

**Examples:**
1. Don't obscure the key risk in the appendix; put it in the executive summary.
2. The vague wording seemed designed to obscure the actual fee structure.

---

### 1121. calculate → quantify

**Meaning:** to express or measure something as a number

**Examples:**
1. We need to quantify the expected savings before presenting to the board.
2. The team struggled to quantify the impact of the brand campaign.

---

### 1122. campaign → endeavor

**Meaning:** a determined attempt or effort toward a goal

**Examples:**
1. The rebranding endeavor took nearly a year to complete.
2. This customer retention endeavor requires input from every department.

---

### 1123. cap → upper bound

**Meaning:** the highest allowable amount for something

**Examples:**
1. The contract sets an upper bound on annual price increases.
2. We set an upper bound on discretionary spending for the quarter.

---

### 1124. capacity → bandwidth

**Meaning:** the amount of resources, time, or ability available to do something

**Examples:**
1. The team doesn't have the bandwidth to take on another project this quarter.
2. We're hiring two analysts to increase bandwidth for the upcoming audit.

---

### 1125. capital → principal

**Meaning:** the main sum of money invested or lent, as opposed to interest

**Examples:**
1. The loan requires monthly repayments of both principal and interest.
2. Investors are more concerned with protecting their principal than chasing high returns.

---

### 1126. capture → harness

**Meaning:** to control and make use of something effectively

**Examples:**
1. The company is working to harness customer data for personalized marketing.
2. We need to harness the momentum from this quarter's strong results.

---

### 1127. cargo → freight

**Meaning:** goods transported by ship, plane, or truck

**Examples:**
1. Rising freight costs are squeezing margins across the shipping industry.
2. The delay was caused by a backlog at the freight terminal.

---

### 1128. carve → delineate

**Meaning:** to clearly define the boundaries or details of something

**Examples:**
1. The new division was created to delineate a clear niche in the enterprise market.
2. The contract delineates each party's responsibilities in detail.

---

### 1129. case → precedent

**Meaning:** an earlier event or decision used as an example or guide for future situations

**Examples:**
1. This settlement sets a precedent for how we handle similar disputes going forward.
2. Legal reviewed past precedent before advising on the contract terms.

---

### 1130. cash → liquidity

**Meaning:** the availability of cash or easily convertible assets

**Examples:**
1. The company maintains strong liquidity to weather any short-term downturn.
2. Tight liquidity forced the firm to delay its planned expansion.

---

### 1131. cast → designate

**Meaning:** to formally assign someone to a role or task

**Examples:**
1. She was designated as the lead for the upcoming compliance review.
2. The board designated a successor ahead of the CEO's retirement.

---

### 1132. casual → nonchalant

**Meaning:** relaxed and unconcerned, sometimes inappropriately so

**Examples:**
1. The vendor's nonchalant response to the missed deadline frustrated the client.
2. A nonchalant attitude toward compliance training raised concerns during the review.

---

### 1133. catalog → inventory

**Meaning:** a complete list of items, such as products in stock

**Examples:**
1. We updated the product inventory ahead of the holiday sales season.
2. The warehouse team conducted a full inventory count at quarter-end.

---

### 1134. category → segment

**Meaning:** a distinct part or group within a larger whole

**Examples:**
1. The premium segment now accounts for a third of total revenue.
2. We are launching a targeted campaign for the small business segment.

---

### 1135. cater → accommodate

**Meaning:** to provide for someone's needs or requirements

**Examples:**
1. The new pricing tiers are designed to accommodate smaller businesses.
2. We adjusted the schedule to accommodate clients in different time zones.

---

### 1136. caution → prudence

**Meaning:** careful and sensible behavior, especially in avoiding risk

**Examples:**
1. The CFO urged prudence before committing to the large capital expenditure.
2. Analysts praised the company's prudence during the economic downturn.

---

### 1137. ceiling → threshold

**Meaning:** an upper limit not to be exceeded, or a barrier to advancement

**Examples:**
1. The pricing threshold cannot be raised without approval from procurement.
2. Many women in the industry still describe hitting a threshold in senior promotions.

---

### 1138. celebrate → commemorate

**Meaning:** to mark or honor an important event or achievement

**Examples:**
1. The company commemorated its tenth anniversary with a company-wide event.
2. We commemorated the successful merger with a joint town hall.

---

### 1139. chain → hierarchy

**Meaning:** a system of ranking people or things according to authority or status

**Examples:**
1. Any exception must be approved through the proper hierarchy of command.
2. The new org chart flattened the reporting hierarchy significantly.

---

### 1140. challenge → headwind

**Meaning:** a factor that creates difficulty or slows progress, often external

**Examples:**
1. Rising interest rates remain a headwind for the housing market.
2. Supply chain disruptions were a major headwind this fiscal year.

---

### 1141. champion → proponent

**Meaning:** a person who actively supports and promotes an idea or cause

**Examples:**
1. The CTO has been the leading proponent of migrating to cloud infrastructure.
2. We need an executive proponent to secure funding for this initiative.

---

### 1142. chapter → phase

**Meaning:** a distinct stage or period in a process or plan

**Examples:**
1. We are entering a new phase of the company's international expansion.
2. The integration phase is expected to take six months.

---

### 1143. character → integrity

**Meaning:** the quality of being honest and having strong moral principles

**Examples:**
1. The company's reputation rests on the integrity of its leadership team.
2. We hire for both competence and integrity during the interview process.

---

### 1144. charge → levy

**Meaning:** to impose a tax, fee, or fine

**Examples:**
1. The government plans to levy a new tax on digital transactions.
2. Late payments will incur a levy of two percent per month.

---

### 1145. charm → charisma

**Meaning:** a compelling attractiveness that inspires devotion in others

**Examples:**
1. The founder's charisma was instrumental in winning over early investors.
2. Great salespeople combine product knowledge with genuine charisma.

---

### 1146. chart → visualize

**Meaning:** to represent information graphically for clarity

**Examples:**
1. We visualized the sales trend to make the quarterly report easier to digest.
2. The analyst visualized customer churn data across each region.

---

### 1147. charter → commission

**Meaning:** to formally authorize or order something to be done

**Examples:**
1. The board commissioned an independent review of the acquisition.
2. Management commissioned a study on employee satisfaction across offices.

---

### 1148. chat → dialogue

**Meaning:** a conversation, especially one addressing a specific topic

**Examples:**
1. We had an open dialogue with the client about the revised timeline.
2. Leadership held a dialogue with staff about the return-to-office policy.

---

### 1149. cheer → rally

**Meaning:** to bring people together in support of a common cause

**Examples:**
1. The CEO rallied the team around the new mission statement.
2. Sales leadership rallied the region around the year-end push.

---

### 1150. childish → puerile

**Meaning:** silly and immature, not suited for a serious context

**Examples:**
1. The client found the joke in the pitch deck puerile and unprofessional.
2. HR flagged the exchange as puerile and out of line with workplace conduct standards.

---

### 1151. chill → unwind

**Meaning:** to relax after a period of stress or work

**Examples:**
1. The offsite gave the team a chance to unwind after a stressful quarter.
2. Management encouraged staff to unwind over the long weekend before the busy season.

---

### 1152. chip → erode

**Meaning:** to gradually wear away or diminish something

**Examples:**
1. Rising costs continue to erode our profit margins each quarter.
2. Repeated delays eroded the client's confidence in our team.

---

### 1153. choice → discretion

**Meaning:** the freedom to decide what should be done in a situation

**Examples:**
1. Managers have discretion to approve expenses under five hundred dollars.
2. Use your discretion when deciding how to handle the escalated complaint.

---

### 1154. chop → truncate

**Meaning:** to shorten something by cutting off part of it

**Examples:**
1. We had to truncate the presentation to fit within the allotted time.
2. The report was truncated to highlight only the most critical findings.

---

### 1155. chore → obligation

**Meaning:** a task someone is required to do, often unpleasant

**Examples:**
1. Filing quarterly compliance reports is a routine obligation for every branch.
2. Employees view mandatory training as an obligation rather than an opportunity.

---

### 1156. chunk → increment

**Meaning:** a small, measured amount by which something increases or is divided

**Examples:**
1. The budget will be released in increments tied to project milestones.
2. We plan to migrate the database in small increments to reduce downtime.

---

### 1157. churn → attrition

**Meaning:** the gradual loss of customers or employees over time

**Examples:**
1. Customer attrition dropped significantly after we launched the loyalty program.
2. High attrition in the support team is driving up training costs.

---

### 1158. cite → invoke

**Meaning:** to mention or use something, such as a rule, as support for an argument

**Examples:**
1. The vendor invoked the force majeure clause to delay delivery.
2. Management invoked the confidentiality policy when declining to comment.

---

### 1159. civil → cordial

**Meaning:** polite and friendly, especially in a formal setting

**Examples:**
1. Negotiations remained cordial despite the disagreement over pricing.
2. We kept the exit interview cordial and professional throughout.

---

### 1160. clarify → elucidate

**Meaning:** to make something clearer by explaining it in detail

**Examples:**
